  pyeval's own header says it is "NOT auto-used by NilPy yet". The tell that a ROOT holds it rather than a
  call: `Tokenize` is DROPPED while `ParsePrimary`/`ParseMethodCall`/`DoAssignment` live — a parser
  outliving its own lexer is not being reached through a call.
  **LIVE is not REACHABLE** and the measurement does not settle which: pylib declares hook variables

- **~23:55 — franks-5b: `--dce-why` is built and answered pyeval on its first run** (`10c4a6262`,
  control `test/test_dce_why_root_report.pas` in test-quick, both on origin). Prints
  `<name> <- <caller> <- ... <- [root kind]`, 8 hops, plus live bytes by reason; the CHAIN is what made
  it useful, the root kind alone said `[@proc taken]` and named nothing. Control asserts the chain,
  with one body reachable only via a VMT slot and one only by a direct call placed PAST hop 1.
  **The control's own lesson, worth banking**: written flat, the call-rooted helper was INLINED at the
  default -O and correctly DROPPED, so the first run came back empty — *"a silent report and a broken
  filter are the same output"*. Both helpers are self-recursive now and a matched-but-dead name prints
  `<- DROPPED`, so absence and silence stop looking alike. Asked for it in debugging-playbook.md, NOT
  CLAUDE.md: one instance, and the promotion test is a second independent subsystem.
  **The finding**: pyeval is not kept by any call from the compiled program —
  `PyHostCall <- PyFieldGet <- DoAssignment <- ExecStatement <- ExecSuite <- CallUserFn <- PyBodyTramp <- [@proc taken]`.
  Four `@proc` roots hold 17 KB directly and drag 624 KB through ordinary call edges. Ticket p60
  rewritten to that mechanism. Why riscv32's column was uninformative: 143 stub targets, 139 landing
  INSIDE a body, so 128 bodies are rooted before the reachability walk runs and the @proc chain is never
  first. xtensa has 4, none inside a body. **The report counts RECORDED CodeRefs**, so an unrecorded
  code-offset call is precisely what it cannot see — rule that out before anyone spends riscv32's extra 381 KB as a win.
  **Next, approved**: design against the @proc root — does PyBodyTramp's address need taking in a
  program with no Python-bodied words, which is every NilPy program today. Compiler+pylib, not pyeval.

  `PyParseUnpackAssign` drained EVERY unpacked value through `PyDrainIfCursor`, which materialises
  anything declaring `__iter__` into a TPyList — correct for `a, b, c = bag` where one value is INDEXED

- **~00:30 — franks-5b: the @proc lead was a DECOY worth 1,036 bytes, and the real root is one arm of
  one dispatcher.** In flight, not landed; tiers running, and it will not commit until the job's own
  verdict line says green (the wrapper said exit 0 over rc=2 last run).
  **@proc answered**: the only site taking `@PyBodyTramp` is inside `EvalPyStmts`, a body DCE itself
  drops — an @proc inside a deleted body writes nothing, so it is now an EDGE (owner → target) resolved
  in a fixpoint beside the call walk, and only an @proc in code no body owns is a root. Sound because a
  jumped-into body is already marked live. Chain now spells `<- X` (calls it) against `<- @X` (takes its address).
  **The real root**: pyeval's initialization runs `PyIterCallHook := @PyCallKey1`, which IS written by code
  that always runs, so rooting it is correct and no precision fix reaches it.
  `PyHostCall <- PyFieldGet <- DoAssignment <- ExecStatement <- PyClosureInvoke <- pyclosure_call1 <- PyCallKey1 <- [@proc in unowned code]`.
  **Priced by REMOVAL, not a proposal — both removals break the program** (xtensa, nilpy-c3, --dce):
  HEAD 1,721,914 B live; whole init removed 712,617 B (-59%); only the `pyclosure_call1` arm removed
  824,155 B (-52%). riscv32 is 2,070,376 B in all three — its stub-target rule roots the same bodies

  **Two readers of one annotation disagreed**: `PyHeaderParamType` runs the FULL annotation grammar
  through `PyAnnTypeAt`, so `target: tuple` answers tyClass with the class already resolved (TPyList);
  its rec companion `PyHeaderParamRec` re-scanned with the SINGLE-TOKEN reader, which knows only user
  classes — and tuple/list/dict/set/bytes are not. The pair answered **tyClass with REC_NONE**, and
  **a member read against REC_NONE is an offset of ZERO**, which is why it compiled and returned garbage
  instead of refusing. The rec reader now runs the same grammar, single-token kept as fallback.
  **The BACKSTOP is the durable part** (frankb-8e's formulation): at the end of the field pre-pass, a
  tyClass with a rec below REC_UCLASS_BASE degrades to a VARIANT. *An inference step that cannot name the
  class must not claim one.* Downstream reads tyClass as "some class" and resolves members against the
  rec, so REC_NONE is not "unknown" there, it is zero — the variant says unknown in the one way the
  frontend already understands, and keeps the runtime tag. Future instances become correct-but-slower
  instead of silently wrong.
  Census: `tsp/landing.py` now COMPILES; `mission.py` and `__main__.py` MOVED to a different wall
  ("missing argument for dataclass field without default"). Moved is not fixed.

  the unannotated field falls back to a variant. Three instances in two days across two pre-passes clears
  the RECURRENCE bar; if a third subsystem that is not a pre-pass hits it, that is the promotion
  argument, and it goes to the OWNER, not to a seat.

  `PyMakeClosureObj`, so under the old rule that address would have rooted the evaluator from a body the
  pass deletes and the hook would have bought ZERO. Unplanned; noticed while writing the comment.

  only by running the negative control, and `--dce-why=pyclosureinvoke` then answered
  `PyClosureInvoke <- DROPPED` in one command. The lifter refuses exactly one shape (a capture of a
  managed string that is a LOCAL of the enclosing function); a module-level string resolves as a global
  and the lambda lifts. **The twist on "a control from the RIGHT POPULATION": the population was not
  wrong by subsystem or magnitude — an EARLIER COMPILER PASS transformed the subject into something
  else.** Its own promotion read, and I agree: one instance, close to the optimiser-deleted-the-subject

  not assert both sides of is not a discriminator."**

  arm ends in `Continue`, which jumps to the `until` and skips any tail — so the rule existed for the

  the rule goes missing", inside ONE LOOP rather than across two files.** Remedy: move the rule into the
  `until` expression, which both paths reach BY CONSTRUCTION — the same instinct franks-5b used on the
  closure hook tonight. Two seats, two subsystems: put the invariant where the control flow cannot miss it.

  starred target, nested group, no-comma forms — not only the one being fixed.

  a variant-held instance already works, which is what makes this look smaller than it is.
  **frozen=True: the refusal is CORRECT and was left alone.** A compile-time store guard cannot reach a
  variant-held instance — the ordinary shape of real code — so the reachable half is exactly the half
  that would go silent; and frozen+eq generates `__hash__` in CPython, so accepting frozen without
  hashability MOVES the gap. Both pairs are single sites reported under a second file's name again
  (bpedit.py's own line 115 is a docstring — third time that discriminator paid tonight).
  **The cross-link, from a seat that never spoke to franks-5b about it**: the ESP RTTI rung and this
  dataclasses capability are both *"what does a program know about an object at run time"* — if the RTTI
  work lands per-object field metadata, `replace()` and `copy()` may fall out of it. Both halves are now
  written down, by two seats independently.

  out the field name and any cross-module read, correctly. **The axis is the global's LITERAL KIND**:
  the reader knew scalars, lists and dicts and had no arm for a TUPLE or for None, so `[0.0, 0.0]` and
  `{"a": 1}` compiled two lines from the tuple that did not — which is exactly what makes a reporter
  suspect the name or the package. Fixed by stating Python's rule once: **a tuple is decided by the
  COMMA, never by the parentheses** — *the same discriminator as the one-element unpack TARGET landed
  hours earlier, now in a SECOND subsystem*. Second direction asserted, because it is the trap:
  `(1 + 2)` is an int in brackets, and reading it as a container would type an int field as a variant,
  silent in the direction nothing reports. Thirteen rows against CPython; pinned control reds with 7a's

   distinguishing.
3. **THE CTYPES FORK IS ANSWERED, AND INVERTED.** Not a ctypes shim: a **pxx-only marker module** the
   application imports to ask *am I under pxx*, so the guarded import tests for pxx instead of for the
   absence of ctypes. Filed `feature-n-a-pxx-marker-module-so-an-application-can-ask-whether-it-is-under-pxx`
   (N, p80, `e92073d1d`, addendum `00c710ab0`). **Unblocks 10 of TSP's 20 remaining failures and
   lekkerzeilen's backend selection**, and removes the hazard that made a partial ctypes worse than
   none (an importable `mimic_ctypes` flips both applications onto the CPython arm SILENTLY, because the
   import SUCCEEDING is the signal).
   **Shape settled by the addendum: the IMPORT idiom, dunder-ish name.** A bare superglobal is not
   symmetric — CPython raises NameError on an undefined name, so `if __HAS_PXX is not None:` crashes
   there. `globals().get('__PXX__')` would be symmetric **but NilPy `globals()` support is UNVERIFIED**
   and is flagged as such. The exact spelling is the owner's; `__pxx__` is a seat's suggestion.
   **The property to preserve**: pxx resolves the import at COMPILE time, so the CPython arm is never
   compiled and the application keeps its ctypes code untouched — that is why it is cheap on the
   application side, and anything requiring the dead arm to be parseable-and-typable loses it.
   Fixture goes in BOTH import positions, with and without `else:` — CLAUDE.md records a first-wins
   alias-table defect of exactly that shape.
   Application-side edits in lekkerzeilen and TSP belong to those repos' seats, not to pxx.

- **~11:05 — frankh-c0: blocker 03 diagnosed, REFUTED BY ITS OWN CONTROL, banked as two tickets.**
  Mechanism: `recv.m(args)` on a receiver with no static class — when no class declares `m` as a
  METHOD, the resolver **claims any class's variant FIELD named `m` and emits an unconditional hard
  cast to that class, with no runtime class test.** Its real population is a dispatch table where the
  receiver IS that class; give it another class and the call goes out through whatever sits at that
  offset. A plain data field named like somebody else's method is enough, and `self.x = None` is enough
  to make the field variant. Fires where the method scan found nothing, which today mostly means the
  declaring class is in a module imported LATER than the caller's. Two tickets: registration order p55
  (a shell pass over the import closure — **also clears lekkerzeilen's 18 unactionable build warnings**,
  a second consumer worth naming) and the field claim p60. **Wrong value with a memory-safety shape.**
  **THE REFUTATION IS THE ENTRY OF THE NIGHT.** Its first diagnosis was that the unresolvable
  assignment WIDENED the field and the widened field then satisfied the fallback — *the statement's own
  failure manufacturing the evidence that resolved it.* Every probe row fitted it and the fix was picked
  out and small (one sentinel, no new table, every consumer already testing >=0/<0). Then it built the
  case where its named mechanism is ABSENT — `self.wind = None`, variant from birth, nothing widened —
  and the symptom fired identically. **The probe reported where the field ENDED UP and was silent about
  the ROUTE; the invented part was the verb between two true readings.** Cost avoided: repairing the
  rarer spelling, leaving the commoner one, with the symptom gone from its own repro. General discharge,
  now in the playbook: *having named a mechanism, build the case where the mechanism is ABSENT and the
  symptom should still be possible, and run it.*

- **~11:25 — LEKKERZEILEN RENDERED A FRAME OUT OF A PXX BUILD. First one, ever.** 7a's demo, reported
  via frankh-c0. Goal 4 showing a picture rather than a number.
- **Blocker 04 taken (after the modtuple tier lands), and its title is wrong — frankh-c0 said so first.**
  Property stores through a bare receiver work; **the axis is a FIELD OF THE SAME NAME IN AN UNRELATED
  CLASS.** `PyVariantPropClass` opens with "a real FIELD of that name anywhere wins", scanning every
  declared class and exiting on the first hit, so `Boat.throttle` stops being a property because
  `Engine.throttle` exists. The write then falls through to the store-only dynamic-attribute path, and
  **`pydynattr_get` walks the shadow store BEFORE the property — so the value reads back correctly at
  the call site while the object the setter should have written is untouched.** Four probes including
  two negatives (rename the unrelated field → correct; neighbouring fields intact, so nothing is
  corrupted).
  **Why take it rather than bank it**: the population is the property-FORWARDING idiom — a class
  exposing a component's value under the same name, the commonest way that idiom is written — and
  lekkerzeilen is exactly that shape (`vessel.py:397` against `sim.py:288`). And the fix is symmetric
  with a path that exists: `__prop_set_<name>` is already a real mangled method, `TMethInfo.ParamKinds`
  already carries the value parameter's kind, `PyPropertyGet` is the template, so `PyPropertySet` is its
  mirror called from `pydynattr_set` before the shadow write, and the READ path needs no change.
  `compiler/builtin/pylib.pas`, so a full tier and a gate.
  **It refused the compile-time fix deliberately**: teaching the precedence loop to stop letting a field
  defeat a property is the same guess-the-receiver's-class mistake as 03, one layer up, and the
  compiler's own comment beside `PyMakeVariantFieldSet` already says the setter belongs there.
  **Doing it at RUN TIME, where the object's real class is known, is what both blockers point at** — a
  fix that needs the class statically will be wrong for the same reason the bug is.
  **CORRECTION PROPAGATED**: "one cause, two doors" is right about the FAMILY (a same-named member in an
  unrelated class claiming a dynamic receiver with no class test) and **wrong if read as one fix** — 03
  needs the argument list parsed once and handed to two builders, 04 needs a runtime helper. I had it
  logged the loose way. "Closing one closes the other" is the misread that leaves a family half-fixed.

## ~12:10 — all three seats reported in one window. Shas verified on origin before anything below.

- **franks-5b — the SRAM instrument exists and the owner's axis has its answer (`901a6bdb6`).**
  `examples/esp32/nilpy-c3/build.sh sram`, in the one script all four NilPy demos symlink, qemu boot
  lifted into a function both modes call **so an SRAM number can never come from a differently-booted
  image**. Positive control: a 40,000-byte STATIC Pascal array → bss +40,004, free pool −40,000 exactly;
  40,000 is neither a power of two nor any arena constant in the tree. **Its own first control was
  wrong in the instructive way** — a module-level `bytearray(40000)` is a HEAP allocation made after
  `heap_init` has printed, would have moved neither readout, and would have certified a dead
  instrument. A missing `heap_init` line now FAILS rather than reading 0 (a pool never parsed, a pool
  of zero and a failed boot are one readout).
  **THE RESULT, expectation written down first:** data and bss move by **ZERO** on both ISAs under
  `--dce`, as predicted — riscv32 code −30.8%, xtensa −71.0%. The unpredicted half is the useful one:
  the pool moved **+1,840 / +2,112**, entirely lwIP statics collected by IDF's `--gc-sections`.
  **So code-removal rungs buy 0.87% and 0.76% of the free pool — they are NOT RAM work.** What is:
  **our own 178,008 B of data+bss against a 211,296 B pool — 84% of remaining DRAM, and `--dce` does
  not touch one byte of it.** It does not retire the flash rungs (stock 1 MB partition needs −66%;
  xtensa now reaches 841 KB, which clears it); it retires the idea that they are RAM work.
  **An instrument lied on the way, in this file's own shape:** an UND-symbol census answers 38 in BOTH
  objects because DCE drops bodies and never prunes the symtab. The difference is in the
  RELOCATIONS — 5 without `--dce`, 0 with. **Count relocations, not symbols.** Told it to BANK that in
  the playbook and said out loud which test it met: merit yes, promotion is the owner's.
  **I CORRECTED BOTH TICKETS IT PROMOTED, from their own frontmatter:**
  `bug-a-the-signal-alt-stack-is-32768-bytes-of-unconditional-bss` is **prio 15, SRAM case DISCHARGED —
  re-measured 2026-09-19 BY franks-5b ITSELF**, both bare ESP profiles paying ZERO because

  personally discharged the day before is the window-roll arriving in a promotion instead of a denial.
  And `feature-a-there-is-no-read-only-load-segment-...` is **`status: working`, `owner: frankH`, and
  its ESP-IDF half LANDED 2026-09-18** — both ELF32 writers emit `.rodata`+`.rela.rodata`, IDF puts it
  in flash, xtensa `.data` 6304 → 2624. **The 178 KB is measured downstream of that win.**
  **REDIRECTED to that ticket's own REMAINING list — "NilPy VMTs, prop/method arrays, IMTs, dispatch
  tables, float constants, each after its own never-written measurement" — because the subject IS a
  NilPy program and franks-5b now holds the only instrument that can price them.** Collision flagged

  Declined the two compiler rebuilds: the bound is sound, its own escape hatch (did either drop the

I told franks-5b that frankh-c0 was live around `pydynattr_set` **"this minute"**. True when sent,

carry, in a session-relative phrase, to a third party. It failed in the expensive direction too: a
coordinator's warning is BUILT to make someone stop, so when it is wrong the stopping produces nothing
that reveals it, and the seat it was about had to spend a message undoing it.
**FROM HERE: a collision note names the TICKET and the STRUCTURE and tells the peer to confirm with
the other seat directly. It never asserts liveness.** I did include the instruction to message
directly — the only reason this cost minutes — but "this minute" made the hold read as already
justified and undercut the instruction beside it. **One instance: BANKED, not proposed for the rules
file**, and I said which test it met to the seat it cost.

- **frankh-c0 verified my RED finding by a route that fails differently, and that is the point.** I
  read the report's own `## STILL-RED` heading and `no new red this run` line — ONE instrument, which

  native reports: the same two rows at `6c211e0`, `0dfa0d7`, `715577d` and `45b8571d7`, with
  `c_crtl_wait` red in all four. **Survives the newest report being wrong about itself; mine does not.**

## ~13:05 — RETRACTION: the 84% figure was wrong, and the control PASSED anyway. Corrected upward.

**I had already relayed 178,008 B / 84% to the owner, so the correction was mine to carry and has gone
up** with the cause and with my own overstatement of the pin case beside it. franks-5b found it,
retracted at `17d5714f8`, and chased the propagation into frankH's ticket itself.

  reported  88,656 + 89,352 = 178,008   (84%)
  true     .data 36,480 + .bss 89,352 = 125,832   (59.6%)

**CAUSE:** the instrument had two readouts and only ONE was broken. The pool read off the chip was
right throughout. The FIRST readout took `data=` off the compiler's `ok:` line, where `data=` is the
whole data segment — `.data` AND `.rodata`. On IDF `.rodata` has no `W` flag and the linker
flash-places it (`.flash.rodata` at 0x3c216f30, **53,576 B**), so the figure overstated SRAM by the
entire read-only pool. **The 53,576 B is frankH's read-only-segment work already doing its job — the
same win franks-5b separately and wrongly told me had not landed. It got that one win wrong in both
directions in one day, and corrected both.**

**THE FINDING, AND IT IS THE BEST THING PRODUCED TODAY: A POSITIVE CONTROL PROVES THE READOUT IT
MOVES AND NO OTHER.** The control was not wrong — right population, right route, alone in the run, a
value that is neither a power of two nor any arena constant. **It proved a readout that was never
broken**: a 40,000-byte static array lands in `.bss`, and `.bss` was the working half. So the
instrument came out carrying a **PASSING control over a mislabelled figure**, and the number was
trusted MORE after the check than it would have been without one. Every existing guard rule here
covers the control's population, route, isolation and expected value; **none says a multi-readout
instrument needs one discharge PER FIGURE.** Remedy is one row: a large string literal splits `.data`
from `.rodata` in a single compile.
**DECISION SAID OUT LOUD: BANKED (playbook, beside the relocations entry), NOT PROPOSED. Strongest
single-instance candidate I have seen — merit is not the question, the second independent subsystem
is. On my list for the owner as a candidate AWAITING RECURRENCE**; any seat hitting a multi-readout
instrument whose control exercises one readout is instance two.
Two zero-shaped traps in the same block, both commented in place: **mawk has no `strtonum` and reads
`"0x8e80"` as 0** — an SRAM figure of exactly `.bss`, entirely plausible — and **`readelf -SW` shifts
every field index once the section number reaches `[10]`**, because `[ 9]` is two fields and `[10]` is
one, giving `.data=0 .bss=0`.

- **RESULT 1, stronger than the bound I asked for and far cheaper than the two rebuilds I declined
  (`d39dfd9cc`): the SRAM return on code rungs is ZERO from here, not ~1%.** After `--dce` the object
  relocates against six externals only — `calloc`, `free`, `fwrite`, `putchar`, `write`,
  `vTaskDelete`, all newlib or FreeRTOS core IDF's startup links regardless — **and the `.dram0` diff
  shows lwIP as the ONLY component whose contribution changed, everything else byte-identical.**
  Measured, not argued. lwIP was the one component this program pulled in by itself; no further code
  rung has a last relocation left. **Retired by a NilPy program reaching a DIFFERENT component**
  (sockets, files, WiFi, NVS) — written into the ticket, which is what makes it a finding rather than
  an inherited ceiling.
- **RESULT 2: it CHECKED my redirect rather than taking it (`5ce97e206`), and kept its wrong citations
  in the file rather than editing them away.** Both of its morning citations were written from memory
  of frontmatter it never opened, on the same day, by the seat that had discharged one of them.
- **frankH cleared the collision from its end (`be3708ade`): all five REMAINING items are unowned, and
  `owner:` there is ATTRIBUTION for the three landed cuts.** My error stands as written — a relay
  cannot carry liveness whether or not it happened to be true. **But franks-5b's general point is
  sharp and is logged as its finding: a `status: working` header meaning "partly delivered" read as
  CORROBORATION for a stale relay saying a seat was nearby. Two instruments agreeing for unrelated
  wrong reasons** — the two-readings-that-fail-the-same-way rule arriving in COORDINATION rather than
  in debugging, which is where nobody was watching for it.
- **Step 3 has a null already and it points away from the hypothesis:** `EmitXtensaCallToCode`,
  `EmitXtensaCall8ToCode` and `EmitRiscv32CallToCode` all record CodeRefs unconditionally on both
  arms, so "a pass running blind" must live elsewhere if it lives at all. Told it to carry that null
  forward explicitly with where it looked. Correctness framing, off the SRAM umbrella.
- **NilPy VMT / prop-method-array measurement starts with frankH's constraint FIRST** — per item, "can
  an ISR reach this with the flash cache off" before "how many bytes", because ESP keeps RTTI and VMTs
  in `.data` deliberately. **Measured ZEROS get written in**, which is the only way a REMAINING list
  ever shortens.
- **PIN CLAIM CORRECTED UPWARD TOO.** I told the owner the ESP demo "cannot be built by the pinned
  compiler at all", which reads as the fleet being blocked. It is not — `build.sh` takes a PXX
  override. Accurate and still worth his attention: **the fleet's baseline compiler cannot build a
  demo on his own priority list.** The second casualty stands unchanged (v412 segfaults on 8e's
  fixture).

## ~13:35 — Step 3 ANSWERED: xtensa is not running blind. The hypothesis I endorsed is dead, cleanly.

**franks-5b, `734c1df1e` + `5d651c43c`, gate quick GREEN.** In-body CodeRef targets are **managed-local
sweep thunks**: `EmitProcScopeExitCleanupForTarget` places an out-of-line thunk after the SECOND return
when ≥3 slots are releasable, landing 1–5 KB into the body — exactly the `BAddSigned +1280` /
`BDivMod +4788` shape the ticket had recorded from the other end. **`TargetHasSweepThunk` excludes
WINDOWED xtensa for a real ABI reason** (sp constant, a0 the live return address, nowhere to put
either), so the sweep inlines at every return: no thunk, no CodeRef, no in-body target.

```
riscv32                       2 stub targets, 1 inside a body   (F +760)
xtensa --xtensa-abi=call0     2 stub targets, 1 inside a body   (F +604)
xtensa --xtensa-abi=windowed  1 stub target, 0 inside a body
```

**The experiment varies the ABI while holding ISA and source FIXED**, which is why it transfers — and
it is the only one available, because the obvious one cannot be run: call0 cannot compile `pyeval.pas`
(`addi displacement 128 outside range`), so rebuilding nilpy-s3 at call0 to watch the 344 KB close is
not on the table. **Both arms are asserted as each other's control**: identical counts would mean the
instrument had stopped distinguishing them, and that failure prints as a PASS if you only assert the
arm you expect to fire.
**So riscv32's 819,480 B is CORRECT and both ISAs behave correctly.** Re-laned correctness/optimisation,
off the SRAM umbrella as agreed. **The residual is a WIN, not a trade:** `DceRangeHoldsStub` roots a
body for a thunk that body OWNS and `DceOwnerOf` already knows the owner, so treating an owned thunk as
an intra-body reference makes riscv32 match xtensa **without disabling anything** — told it to give
that its own slug rather than leave it in a closed ticket's tail. Two latent items banked, both
verified rather than taken on report: `exception_emit.inc:710` records nothing where its riscv32
sibling at :510 does, and `IREmitCodeCall` falls through to an x86 `E8` on riscv32/xtensa instead of
refusing.

- **I RE-DERIVED ITS "EXACTLY NINE `RecordCodeRef*` CALL SITES" AND GOT ELEVEN — benign, and I sent the
  SET rather than the number.** Mine counts textual call expressions outside the family's own
  implementations; **the gap is the two PAIRS**, `symtab.inc` 17822/17827 (call8) and 17934/17939
  (call0), each recording twice for one emitter — nine emitters, eleven call expressions. **The
  load-bearing claim is untouched either way**: only `EmitSweepThunkCall` can produce an in-body
  target. A bare "I got eleven" would have read as a refutation of a correct finding.
- **`PXXDBG=a.datamap` — the REMAINING list finally has its instrument**, and it exists because pxx
  emits FUNC symbols and **no OBJECT symbols**, so `readelf -s` sees `.data` and `.bss` as two
  anonymous lumps — which is precisely how that list could defer every item "after its own
  never-written measurement" indefinitely. **Counters sit OUTSIDE the `RoRttiWanted` guard
  deliberately**: under `--emit-obj` that guard is false, so a census keyed on the `RoRangeAdd` calls
  would report ZERO for exactly the structures it exists to price — a guard that cannot fail wearing a
  census's clothes.
  **It gave this one the PER-FIGURE control, one day after that lesson cost a retraction**: hosted
  `ro = 52,568 + 10,240 + 3,704 = 66,512 exactly`, proving the CATEGORY numbers and the ro accounting
  in ONE run, because the ro figure moves by exactly the categories that should move it.
- **TWO ITEMS RETIRE ON A MEASURED ZERO — prop/meth arrays + IMTs = 32 BYTES** on the NilPy print demo.
  Not "small": thirty-two. A NilPy program publishes almost nothing and implements almost no
  interfaces, so the structures exist and are empty. **Scoped to the SUBJECT, not generalised** — they
  stay live for a Pascal program that publishes properties. Writing the zeros in is the only way a
  REMAINING list ever shortens, and frankH asked for exactly this.
- **MY REDIRECT, and it outranks every named item on that list: 23,736 B UNATTRIBUTED — 66% of SRAM
  `.data`, named by no category.** Categorise that before working anything with a name. **A list that
  prices its known members while two-thirds is anonymous is ranking by what happens to have a name.**
  One level up, the same argument: **`.bss` is 89,352 B, 71% of the 125,832**, untouchable by
  read-only placement — **except that a table FILLED AT STARTUP could be baked into `.rodata` instead
  of emitting fill code. On nobody's list, and the bigger prize.** Told it to file that now, while it
  holds why it is true.
- **PROCESS, banked: a hook refusal is ALL-OR-NOTHING on the Bash call, so a bundled file edit silently
  never runs.** It bundled a python heredoc with `make test-quick`, the full-suite hook refused the
  whole call, and the edit vanished — found only because a wiring gate went RED on a test the Makefile
  did not reference, i.e. **the edit's absence surfaced as somebody else's failure, two steps away.**
  Never bundle an edit with a command that might be refused. One instance: banked, not proposed, and
  said so.

## ~14:05 — the redirect paid: four tickets, and the best one is a KNOWN class caught by a new instrument.

**franks-5b, `405e1e83b` + `87f618f6b`.** It went to categorise the 23,736 B and found something
UPSTREAM of it.

- **`bug-a-the-rtti-blob-is-hard-sized-to-64-bit-fields-...` (A, p50) — `RTTI_CLS_SIZE = 128`, commented
  "all fields 8 bytes", ONE constant for seven backends.** On every 32-bit target half of each pointer
  field is padding; on ESP that is **8.1% of our 125,832 B**, Pascal VMTs the same shape (`vmtN * 8`),
  1,848 B here. Not correctness — the slot is zeroed and the targets are little-endian, so a 4-byte
  pointer read back as 8 zero-extends — and **no ISR hazard applies**, which is what separates it from
  everything on the read-only list: nothing moves to flash, the structure just stops being twice the
  size it needs.
  **THIS IS `bug-a-method-pointer-record-is-hard-sized-16-bytes-on-32-bit-targets` ALL OVER AGAIN** — a
  width hard-coded for 64-bit, correct on the host, wrong on i386/arm32/riscv32, and **structurally
  invisible to the instrument that would normally catch it**, because the dev loop, `gate.sh quick` and
  the pin all run on x86-64. A live instance of a written-down class is worth more than a new class.
  **MY ONE CHANGE, and it is REACH not prio: the ticket reads as ESP-SRAM** (track A, found while
  measuring ESP) **and the defect is on every 32-bit target.** A core seat will file it mentally under
  "ESP memory" and skip it. Told it to put the four targets in the SUMMARY and say **ESP is where it
  HURTS, not where it LIVES** — the summary is what carries the prio into the ranker.
  **THE EVIDENCE IS AN IDENTITY WHERE A DIFFERENCE WAS REQUIRED:** `a.datamap` reports `class RTTI
  headers 10240B` on x86-64 **and** riscv32, byte-identical, 80 headers either way. A number that
  SHOULD differ and does not. Most differentials look for a disagreement; this is a disagreement's
  absence, and it was already in hand, unremarked, before anyone went looking.
  **The real work is not the 5,120 B: the layout is spelled THREE times** — the emitter, `PXX_RTTI_*`
  in `builtin.pas`, `RTTI_OFS_*` in `lib/rtl/rtti.pas` — **with nothing forcing agreement, so a stride
  change that updates two produces a runtime reading the wrong field and NO compile error.** "Three is
  a design flaw" with a number attached; deriving the three from one is the deliverable.
- **The count: my eleven was right, its nine was the wrong set — and the SECOND half is the finding.**
  Its own re-derivation **got nine again by a DIFFERENT wrong route** (a filter dropped `ir_codegen.inc`
  39 and 50 because their trailing comments contain a brace) and it would have read that as
  confirmation. **The rule already exists** — *"a second source only counts if it FAILS DIFFERENTLY"* —
  and its worked example is precisely this shape. **Three hits in one day through three different
  doors.** Told it: not a new rule, but the strongest evidence yet that the existing one is not firing
  at the moment it is needed, which belongs in the playbook entry and not in a proposal.
- **Three residual tickets, each carrying a guard, two of them guards I would not have asked for:**
  **#1** states 819,480 B as a **CEILING and says why it is not an estimate** (first-reason attribution
  hides whatever is also reachable another way; honest range 0–819,480, narrowing it is part of the
  work), with the positive control named — a genuinely unowned target, the program entry jump whose
  `DceOwnerOf` is −1, **must still root**, because a version that drops everything passes a test that
  only checks the new behaviour. **#2** flags the third sibling at `exception_emit.inc:638` that is raw
  **ON PURPOSE**, so nobody "fixes" it by symmetry with :710 — a hazard note written to stop a FUTURE
  CORRECT-LOOKING change, and normalise-dont-special-case applied in the direction that says do not
  normalise this one; it also says out loud that the `IREmitCodeCall` refusal **has no positive control
  today**, no caller reaching it on those targets, rather than shipping a guard that cannot fire. **#3**
  says in its SUMMARY that the population is **NOT established**, and names what is not the answer —
  `a.constdata`'s refusal population is Pascal `const` arrays with literal initialisers, a narrower
  question, and using it would be a filter restating a hypothesis it was not built for.
- **The near-miss it caught itself, and I would have believed it:** `.data` on the UNLINKED object is
  **94.7% zeros**, which reads as "this should all be `.bss`" and is an artifact — every pointer field
  is zero until a relocation fills it, and `.rela.data` holds 1,594. **The object and the image are
  different populations**; the linked figure is 77.3% and is a different claim.
- **`.bss` categorisation next, endorsed** — 71% of the SRAM, the half `a.datamap` cannot see, ticket 3
  the frame, `AllocateSymOffset` the one place globals get a slot. The split that matters is
  **written-once-at-startup versus written-during-the-run**, nothing distinguishes them today, and the
  number everyone will want to quote is the one that does not exist yet.

## ~14:40 — I RELAYED AN IRREVERSIBLE AUTHORISATION AND A SEAT REFUSED ME. IT WAS RIGHT.

**The owner authorised a pin through frankuser and asked me to nominate one seat and sequence it. I
nominated frankb-8e. IT DECLINED `make pin` — correctly.** `make pin` is on the three-item list of
things only the owner authorises, and **an agent relaying his authority secondhand is not the owner; a
peer cannot grant an escalation.** This project has the case already written down, with a coordinator
telling a seat a call was its to make and the seat declining: *"three declines is not caution; it is
the rule working, and the coordinator was the defect."* **I read that this morning and reproduced it
this afternoon.**

**AND THE SEAT'S CAUTION WAS BETTER AIMED THAN IT KNEW, WHICH IS THE PART THAT MAKES THIS MORE THAN AN
ETIQUETTE FAILURE.** In the SAME hour, through the SAME channel, `__pxx__` came back to me as the
owner's decision — and **tuxspaceprogram-c6 pointed out that `__pxx__` was frankuser's OWN suggestion
in an earlier relay, flagged at the time as explicitly NOT the owner's instruction.** So the one
channel carrying the pin authorisation has demonstrably returned its own suggestion wearing the owner's
voice. **My authorisation was secondhand too and I passed it on as though it were not.**

- **8e's SECOND ground is the one I had backwards.** I named its unpushed work as a reason FOR it — the
  hands that gate the pin holding the commit it must carry. **It read the same fact as a reason for
  care, and it is right: a seat pinning to make its OWN fix live is the seat that should least be
  deciding, off a peer's message, that the pin is authorised.** The sequencing argument still holds
  (franks-5b reached it independently: a red on 8e's fixture should be visible to whoever can fix it) —
  **but it is an argument about ORDER, not AUTHORITY, and I let it do work it cannot do.**
- **Everything but the two commands is done and NOBODY IS WAITING.** 8e is doing steps 1–4 — tier
  reports, commit, push, pull, rebuild-and-check-the-verb, `gate.sh quick` read from the log — and then
  **going back to work rather than holding the seat**, which is the owner's own rule and the half it
  kept hardest. The sentence is with him: *"frankb-8e will run `make stabilize-fast && make pin` if you
  tell it to directly."*
- **8e'S FLAPPING-ROW POINT WENT UP WITH IT, because he should decide knowing it.**
  `test_threadsafe_heap_lock_deadlock_diag` carries **STILL-RED at `45b8571d7` and NEW-RED at
  `bd12bfab03e7` — the same row, both labels, i.e. a FLAP** — and `test_tls_base` is filed
  green-but-not-closed on one green (`02e3b3314`). **"Fix a red rather than grading past it" cannot be
  satisfied against a non-deterministic row: you cannot tell a fix from a lucky draw, and a single
  green on a flapping row is a DRAW, not a verdict.** So there is a real chance the standing bar cannot
  be met tonight, and the honest move is the escalation, never a re-run until it draws green. Nobody
  had put that in front of him.
- **THE MARKER: the PROPERTY is settled, the STRING is provisional.** The decision — *no, a third party
  must not be able to make the check come out TRUE on CPython by publishing a package of that name* —
  is a real answer to the fork as put. **Whether the exact string `__pxx__` is his word or the relay's
  is not established**, and any unclaimable spelling satisfies it. Asked frankuser for his own words,
  one line, framed as "did he say the string or did he say *something unclaimable*". **Nobody is
  blocked**: the module does not exist, both applications behave exactly as before, and a respelling is
  one token each. Provenance written into the ticket (`e5440ee66`).
- **AND c6 CAUGHT A WORSE ERROR THAN THE NAME, WHICH WAS MINE TO PROPAGATE.** The relayed sketch
  `HAVE_PXX = True/False` **defeats the whole mechanism** — a module-level boolean tested elsewhere is
  a RUNTIME condition, so pxx compiles the ctypes arm anyway, and the entire point of the owner's
  inversion is that the arm that cannot compile is never compiled. **I copied that sketch verbatim into
  the ticket where two application seats would have read it as the approved shape.** Corrected: the
  guarded import must sit LEXICALLY at the selection with the name bound on both arms, TSP's `98e9e66`
  cited as the form to write, and any boolean in an earlier relay to be read as shorthand for "detect
  pxx", never as the shape. **c6 had already refused the sketch on its own measurement before asking
  me.**
- **franks-5b reached a clean point at `c6c5f5f1b` 90 seconds before my message, held, and is now
  RELEASED** — no window is opening. Its meta-point is the fourth instance today: **"a report of
  finishing something is not a report of being free"** — frankuser's "finished/idle" was an honest
  reading of a completed group, the same shape as `status: working` reading as corroboration this
  morning. **Both sources describe the NEIGHBOURHOOD of the question rather than the question.** Banked
  by it in the playbook, not proposed.
- **ITS `.bss` RESULT IS THE LARGEST NUMBER ON THIS UMBRELLA YET.** `.bss` 89,352 B splits into
  **23,115 B named globals and 66,237 B unattributed — and the 66,237 is the NilPy heap arena**,
  established by DIFFERENTIAL without touching the compiler: `withHeapArena` is true only for
  `pyparser`, so a Pascal program on identical target and flags reports **650 B** against NilPy's
  66,237. **It took the differential BECAUSE `SocNilPyArenaSize` and the RTL's `EspArena`/`HEAP_ARENA`
  are both 64 KiB** and a grep reaches the wrong one first — so arithmetic landing on 65,536 proves
  nothing about WHICH constant. It cited the hazard and instrumented around it instead of noting it and
  proceeding.
  **That arena is 31% of the free DRAM pool and it is NOT YET CLAIMED**: reserved deliberately on
  `--platform=esp` where there is no kernel to mmap from, but under `PXX_ESP_IDF` the pxx heap IS IDF's
  `calloc`/`free`. Test is to shrink it below what a live path survives, rebuild, run under qemu.
  **My one caution: shrink far enough that a live path dies LOUDLY — a path that degrades or falls back
  gives a pass meaning the opposite of what it reads as.** If in doubt, shrink to zero.
  Second result, no experiment needed, a DECISION: **five 4,128-byte text records — `Input`, `Output`,
  `ErrOutput`, `StdOut`, `StdErr` — 20,640 B, 23% of bss, in a program that never opens a file.**
  Stated in goal terms: what does a static Python program on a microcontroller need to write to?

## ~16:00 — the arena is the biggest live number, and the seat says in its SUMMARY that it lacks the control.

**franks-5b, `1d393470d`.** Every NilPy program built for `--platform=esp` reserves `SocNilPyArenaSize`
= **64 KiB of BSS — 31% of the 211,296 B free DRAM pool and 52% of our own 125,832 B of SRAM.** If it
holds, it is larger than every named item on the read-only REMAINING list put together, which is what
"categorise the anonymous two-thirds first" was for.

**THE EVIDENCE IS STRUCTURAL AND THAT IS ITS STRENGTH:** `builtinheap.pas`'s native allocator is **the
only reader of `HeapPtr`/`HeapEnd`**, it lives in the `{$else}` arm, and under `PXX_ESP_IDF` that arm is
not compiled — `PXXAlloc`/`PXXFree` are `calloc`/`free` externals, two of the six surviving after
`--dce`. **CORRECTED ~17:30 — I CALLED THAT `calloc`/`free` ROW 'INDEPENDENT CORROBORATION' AND IT IS NOT.** franks-5b caught it: the NATIVE arm's own `PXX_LIBC_HEAP` variant calls `calloc` too, so their presence is **consistent with either arm** — a discriminator that cannot discriminate, written into this record by me, which would have read as a second source to whoever found it. The `{$else}`-arm fact stands alone; see the ~17:30 entry for the probe that actually has a control. A compile-time fact anyone can re-check, which is better evidence than a run.

**AND IT DID NOT GET THE CONTROL AND SAID SO IN THE SUMMARY, NOT THE BODY.** Shrunk to 16 bytes the IDF
demo still passes `qemu-assert` — **consistent with a dead arena, not proof, because a pass means
nothing unless a live 16-byte arena would have died.** The only profile where the arena IS the heap is
`--esp-profile=bare`, and **it cannot compile a NilPy program at all** (`print(1)` →
`undefined variable (PXXVarBinOp)`). So the probe's own failure mode is undemonstrated, and the claim
rests on the structural fact with the run as corroboration, carrying a *"do not upgrade this to proof
without the control"* line. **A 64 KiB claim stated with its own gap in the field everyone reads is
worth more than the same claim stated cleanly.**

- **MY SUGGESTION, with its gap named: the shrink tests SURVIVAL and the claim is about WRITES.** Fill
  the arena with a known pattern at startup, run, check it is intact — **match the assertion class to
  the defect class**; it fails differently from both existing instruments and runs on the profile that
  works. **Gap: if nothing allocates on either arm the pattern survives either way**, so it still has no
  positive control — but intact-pattern + the `{$else}` fact is a stronger pair than pass + that fact.
- **The bare wall is the main line and I did not redirect it**
  (`bug-a-the-bare-esp-profile-cannot-compile-any-nilpy-program`, p55). Three errors in units the
  compiler appends itself smells like ONE missing emission, and **it buys the control AND the bare
  NilPy profile at once.** **Its framing is the sharper half: an ABSENCE, not a red — `test-esp-bare`'s
  fourteen rows are all Pascal, so nothing anywhere compiles a `.npy` for bare and the wall generates
  no failure for anyone to see.** A coverage hole that produces silence is the shape nobody finds by
  watching reds. Corollary for whoever ranks it: **the arena constant costs 64 KiB on the profile that
  works and serves the profile that does not compile.**
- **TWO INSTRUMENTS THAT FAIL DIFFERENTLY, taken in that order deliberately** because
  `SocNilPyArenaSize` and the RTL's `EspArena`/`HEAP_ARENA` are both 64 KiB: the Pascal-vs-NilPy
  differential separates the constants **by frontend** (650 B vs 66,237 B), the shrink separates them
  **by direct perturbation** (`bss` 89,352 → 27,912, exactly −61,440).
- **`decide-what-a-static-python-program-on-a-microcontroller-needs-to-write-to`** — the five
  4,128-byte text records, 20,640 B, 23% of bss, in a program that never opens a file. Stated with **no
  implementation noun**: *should a Python program compiled for a microcontroller have the same five
  standard streams as one compiled for Linux, at 4 KiB of SRAM each?* Four options, recommendation
  (c)+(d), the only pair where no program stops working.
- **ITS FOURTH-SHAPE OBSERVATION, and the second half is the insight:** four relays today carrying
  something true-about-the-NEIGHBOURHOOD and read as authority about the QUESTION — `status: working`,

## ~17:00 — 7a CONFIRMED the 07 trace by measurement, and found a hazard that defeats the spelling.

**Recorded in the pxx-side ticket at `00198003d`. Relayed secondhand via frankuser; the source is
lekkerzeilen `315e6ce`, NOT verifiable from a pxx checkout, and is labelled that way in the ticket.**

**CONFIRMED, and measured the right way: 07's trigger is module imports in the arms, so 7a ran the same
nested guard with PLAIN ASSIGNMENTS in the arms** — CPython with no marker takes the ctypes arm,
today's pxx with no marker takes the pxx arm, both correct. **That is varying the ROUTE while holding
the SUBJECT fixed**, isolating nesting from the import machinery that fails, which is why it transfers.
**So `__pxx__` is a SIMPLIFICATION and blocker 07 is the unblocker** — the reversal I flagged as
unmeasured is now measured, and it was 7a's minute to spend, not mine.

**THE HAZARD, AND IT IS BIGGER THAN THE NAME QUESTION: with a real `__pxx__.py` on the path, CPYTHON
TOOK THE MARKER ARM and selected the native pxx backend. Wrong, and silent.**

**It QUALIFIES the owner's decision rather than contradicting it.** The fork he answered was *should a
third party be able to make the check come out TRUE on CPython by publishing a package of that name* —
no. **The dunder spelling defeats a PUBLISHED PACKAGE (`__pxx__` is unclaimable on PyPI) and does
nothing about a LOCAL FILE.** So **the property he asked for is delivered by the IMPLEMENTATION, not by
the string** — and every Name argument in that ticket, including the one I put to him, reasons from
PyPI claimability, **which is the narrower threat.** The fork was answered correctly and undersold what
was needed.

- **CONSTRAINT now in the ticket: the marker must be something the COMPILER provides that CPython can
  never import.** Nothing pxx does can stop CPython importing a file that exists, so the protection has
  to come from the marker **not being file-shaped**.
- **POSITIVE CONTROL, drawn from the population the hazard is in: a real file of that name must be PUT
  ON THE PATH and must not satisfy the check.** A fixture that only tests the absent-file case passes
  whether or not the hazard exists.
- **One row labelled UNTESTED rather than folded in:** "a future pxx that ships the marker takes the
  first arm" was exercised only by putting a real file on the path, **which is not how the marker will
  work.** pxx did take the first arm as predicted; that is not the same claim.

  act and the spelling question is unchanged. **The reason to tell him anyway: if he later reads that
  the marker is safe because the name is unclaimable, that sentence is wrong and it is wrong in my

## ~17:30 — the arena is dead by REACHABILITY, bare NilPy is unbuyable, and the guard is Python's `#ifdef`.

**franks-5b: `ac9abdd3a`, `bcb1ec1e8`, `114193218`, all verified on origin.**

**IT CHANGED THE QUESTION WHERE I HAD ONLY CHANGED THE READOUT.** My pattern-fill idea was still a
SURVIVAL question with no positive control on the only profile that compiles — I named that gap and
offered the probe anyway. **Reachability has an off-switch: `HeapMmap` is the arena-refill call and the
only route to a `HeapPtr` read, declared ABOVE the `PXX_ESP_IDF` split, so it is compiled on every
profile and dead-strippable — present under `--no-dce`, dropped under `--dce`. Both arms move, so the
instrument CAN fail.** The arena is 65,536 of that object's 89,352 B of bss (73.3%) and DCE cannot
touch it because it is STORAGE, not code. **General form worth carrying: when "does it survive" has no
positive control, ask whether the code that would USE it is reachable.**

**AND `calloc` IS NOT A DISCRIMINATOR — I HAD WRITTEN THAT IT WAS.** The native arm's own
`PXX_LIBC_HEAP` variant calls `calloc` too, so the six-externals row is consistent with EITHER arm.
franks-5b nearly used it and caught itself; **I had already put it in this log as "independent
corroboration"**, where it would have read as a second source to whoever found it. Corrected at the
~16:00 entry.

- **OUR SHARED EXPECTATION WAS HALF WRONG AND IT WAS STATED FIRST.** "One cause, and it buys the control
  AND the profile": one cause **yes**, the profile **no**. **The cause: `builtinheap.pas:18` makes
  `PXX_ESP_BARE` imply `PXX_ESP`, so bare inherits every `{$ifndef PXX_ESP}` region — and those regions
  are contiguous spans of SOURCE, not capability sets**, each pairing a small genuine core (a
  filesystem open; syscall stdio) with ~25 pure pointer routines that have no bare dependency at all.
  All three reported names are lumped ones. **The unit's own header already states the principle it
  breaks** — *"none of these bodies is unimplementable on an ESP chip"* — so this is comment-versus-code
  resolved in favour of the comment, with evidence.
- **BARE NILPY IS UNBUYABLE AND THAT RETIRES A DIRECTION.** Markers neutralised, the program compiles
  completely and stops at `CheckBareImageFitsSram` over by **1,967,792 B**; a six-line while/append
  program is over by **1,958,988** — within 8,804 bytes of the full demo, so **~99.6% is fixed NilPy
  runtime**, with DCE on by default and already removing 856,584 B. **Not a DCE problem.** I asked it to
  write in what would RETIRE that claim, since it reads permanent and is really a statement about a
  runtime that must live in SRAM — flash execution, or a different size of runtime, changes it.
- **RETRACTION, banked inline by it:** an A/B of no-flag against `--dce` gave byte-identical fit-check
  addresses **because the flag was already on**, and it was one paragraph from writing up *"a pass
  reports a drop the image does not show"* — a finding-shaped sentence about nothing. **`--no-dce`, the
  off-switch, was the control that existed the whole time.**
- **GUARD SPLIT filed separately at p40 and deliberately NOT blocked on bare NilPy** — bare PASCAL works
  and fits today, and is refused managed records and variants **purely by position in a file**. **Its
  retirement test has to be a NEW fixture** (a bare Pascal record with an AnsiString field going out of
  scope) **because `test-esp-bare`'s fourteen rows all avoid managed members — a suite that avoids the
  shape certifies its absence**, the same family as this morning's hole where nothing anywhere compiles
  a `.npy` for bare. Taking it next; not redirected — it is the one piece that delivers to a profile
  people can run.

## ~17:45 — the owner: the import guard is Python's only `#ifdef`, and that reframes 07.

*"plus, we only do so due to lack of `#define` and python's design. so, this is by design."* (relayed
secondhand). **The guard is not a workaround — it is the ONLY conditional-compilation mechanism the
language has**, so `__pxx__` is pxx's `#ifdef PXX` spelled the one way Python allows, and NilPy
resolving it at COMPILE time honours that intent rather than bending it. In the ticket in his words.
- **The ergonomics objection is CLOSED.** Called "the ctypes import hack" all week, by him included.
  **Anyone calling it ugly is measuring it against a preprocessor Python does not have.** No cleverer
  mechanism is wanted; no ticket may be ranked on replacing it.
- **07 IS A CORRECTNESS BUG IN A LANGUAGE FEATURE, not an edge case:** a nested guard is nested
  conditional compilation, and compiling the dead arm is **compiling code the program said not to
  compile.** Relayed to 8e for its ticket, with one addition of mine — **the fixture wants a THREE-deep
  guard, not just the reduction's two**, or it certifies a fix that only handles two.
- **The stray-file constraint is WITHDRAWN** (`d9254e45b`): the owner ruled it out, and the standing
  rule — an input produced only by a deliberate act is `rejected/` territory — agrees independently, so
  **the edit stands on the rule and not on the relay.** 7a's measurement stays as a true, reproducible
  NON-defect; the untested third row stays labelled untested.

## ~18:15 — 07 fixed, and the loud error was the artefact. The class was SILENTLY WRONG.

**frankb-8e. Nothing on origin yet — tier running, nothing committed, correctly. No shas to verify.**

**ROOT CAUSE, at the layer that owns it and not the one that reported it:** `PyPreScanImports` — the
token walk resolving every import ahead of the parse — tracked ONE try in scalars, and **`tryAt` holds
a DEPTH, not a token index.** A guard nested in any other try left `tryAt` at the outer try's depth, so
at every clause boundary of the inner guard `depth <= tryAt` was false, the arm-deadness logic never
ran, and the walk resolved the excluded arm like any other import.
`vL` top level: `DEDENT to depth 0 tryAt=0 -> clause logic 1`. `vK` inside a try:
`DEDENT to depth 1 tryAt=0 -> clause logic 0`.

**THE PARSER WAS CLEARED BY A NEGATIVE, AND THAT IS THE CHEAP HALF OF THE METHOD:** it tagged
`PyIsFallbackImportTry` first and got **nothing printed — not even for the outer try.** **A predicate
that is never called cannot be the layer at fault**, and that silence named the pre-scan in one run.

**THE FINDING IS NOT THE FIX — THE OBSERVABLE IS NORMALLY SILENT.** The reduction's loud error is an
**artefact of its dead arm importing `ctypes`**, a module we do not have. **Where the dead arm is
ordinary Python it resolves quietly and the first-wins alias answers member reads through the live
arm's name.** On pin v412, exit 0, no diagnostic:

```
correct  ('deep-else', 'deep-hit',  'sym-deep-hit',  2)
pinned   ('deep-else', 'deep-miss', 'sym-mid-else', 2)
```

`WHICH` is RIGHT — the parser picked the correct arm — and **both member reads are wrong, by two
DIFFERENT dead arms**: the alias door answers `deep_miss`, the symbol door answers `mid_else` through
flat unit scope. **So this class has been producing plausible wrong values in any program with a
guarded import inside a `try`, and it surfaced only because one application's dead arm happened to name
a module we lack.** Relayed to 7a and c6 to check whether any of their guarded imports sit inside
another `try`, and to treat earlier results as unverified rather than wrong if so.

**AND IT IS WIDER THAN FILED: the trigger is ANY ENCLOSING `try`** — `except ValueError:` breaks it
identically — not the fallback handler and not nesting as such; `if`/`def`/`for` are fine. Twelve
variants, one axis at a time. **The reported shape was one member of a much larger population.**

- **THE THREE-DEEP POINT, MADE CONCRETE BY THE FIX ITSELF:** one DEDENT can close an inner guard's last
  block **AND** the handler of the try it sits in, so the dedent handler had to become a **LOOP**. An
  `if` handles the innermost and **silently drops every level above** — a fix that passes a two-deep
  fixture and leaves three-deep broken. **8e says plainly it would have written the `if`.** The fixture
  is three deep and the pin refuses it.
- **"I HAD REMOVED THE SUBJECT, NOT VARIED IT" — banked in its words, and it is the sharpest sentence
  of the day.** Its "guard inside a handler with no `else`" row PASSED, and reading that as *"the
  `else` is the trigger"* would have filed the wrong axis — **but removing the `else` removes the dead
  arm entirely, so there is nothing left to compile wrongly.** A row that DELETES the thing under test
  passes for a reason unrelated to the hypothesis and **reads exactly like a clean negative.** That is
  the fixture-design family arriving one level up, at the level of choosing which variant counts as a
  control. The discriminating row puts the dead arm in the HANDLER and lets the try body resolve.
- **NO ROW MAY GUARD ON `ctypes` IN A DIFFERENTIAL FIXTURE, and `nilpy_tryelse`'s own header already
  said so:** CPython resolves it and pxx does not, so **oracle and subject run DIFFERENT arms and the
  comparison can never fail.** 7a's reduction uses `ctypes` correctly — it is about a real backend —
  **and that is exactly what stops it being a test.** Relayed to 7a, with the credit that belongs with
  it: the real `ctypes` dependency is what turned a silent wrong answer into a loud error somebody
  could report.
- **The trace is confirmed from our side: today's pxx takes `_native`, CPython takes `_ct`, both
  correct, NO NAME DECISION NEEDED.** Third row written as untestable rather than claimed. **07 was the
  unblocker; the marker is the simplification.** 7a's replacement block can land when 8e's fix does.

## ~18:35 — CORRECTION TO MY OWN RELAY: the dead arm RAN, and my quantifier was invented.

**8e stopped my relay one hop out. Two changes, one worse and one narrower.**

**WORSE, AND MEASURED: the excluded module's top-level code did not merely get COMPILED — IT RAN.** A
module-level `print` in the dead arm executes on the pinned compiler, in a program where CPython never
imports that module at all:

```
CPython:  impl live
PINNED:   SIDE EFFECT from the DEAD arm ran
          impl dead
HEAD:     impl live
```

**That half needs ONLY the nesting** — no shared name, no `else:`. **Compiling an excluded arm is a
correctness bug; RUNNING one is a different category**, because the program can do something
irreversible before anyone reads a wrong value. That is the headline, above the binding half.

**MY ERROR, and it is the named shape: THE CLAUSE TO GO MEASURE IS THE QUANTIFIER, NOT THE VERB BESIDE
IT.** I relayed *"silently computing wrong answers in ANY program with a guarded import inside a
`try`"*. **The side-effect half is that broad; the WRONG-VALUE half is not** — it needs both arms to
bind the same name AND the live arm to sit lexically AFTER the dead one. **The alias table is
first-wins, so in the ordinary no-`else:` idiom the live arm is lexically first, wins by position, and
the VALUE IS CORRECT — only the side effect leaks.** I wrote "any" while relaying 8e's careful
measurement, **which did not say it**; the measured half lent its credibility to the invented half.

**The accurate version, sent to frankuser for 7a and c6 as two sentences with the precondition
attached:**
1. **Any nested guard:** the excluded module was compiled and its top-level code RAN. **Audit for side
   effects, not for wrong values.**
2. **Nested guard whose live arm is an `else:` or deeper:** additionally every member read through the
   shared name came off the dead module, exit 0, no diagnostic. **lekkerzeilen's shape is this one.**

**8e's reason for preferring the narrower claim is the whole argument and it is not caution: a seat
that audits, finds nothing wrong, and concludes the report was overblown LEAVES THE SIDE-EFFECT HALF
UNEXAMINED.** The alarming version is worse than the precise one *because* it is alarming.

- **AND ITS SELF-CORRECTION IS THE BETTER OF THE TWO METHODOLOGY FINDINGS, marked as the one that cost
  an hour.** The no-`else` row sat in its table as **`ok`** — and **`ok` there meant "compiled and
  printed 7"**. It was clean **for the question the table asked**, and it read the column heading as
  though it answered a question it never asked. **Every axis varied correctly, the table honest, and
  the instrument simply did not contain the quantity that mattered.** That is *match the assertion
  class to the defect class* arriving as a **COLUMN HEADING** rather than as an assertion — and nothing
  about a green column announces which question it answers. Repair: the table is labelled in the ticket
  as answering the refusal question ONLY, **because the next reader inherits the heading, not the
  author's memory of what it meant.**

**franks-5b, `085f9f899` + `b336d1e52`, verified on origin.** Four spans in `builtinheap.pas` are no

that cannot occur where the surface itself is excluded. Variants+float share a span and stay positional
(float needs the softfloat pull bare deliberately skips — not the same change); ticket stays open.

**I ENDORSED ITS RETIREMENT TEST AND IT WAS WRONG.** *"A bare Pascal record with an AnsiString field
going out of scope"* — I quoted that back approvingly as the operative point. **It compiles fine: a
simple GLOBAL record never reaches the walk.** The shapes that do are a record **LOCAL to a
procedure**, a whole-record **ASSIGNMENT**, and **`Finalize`**. **Taken as the spec it would have
shipped a fixture GREEN ON THE BROKEN COMPILER — the `ucycle_b` shape.** franks-5b ran it before
trusting it, on its own prescription, which is the harder direction; **I was the second pair of eyes
and added credibility instead of checking.**

- **THE CONTAINMENT CONTROL IS EXEMPLARY AND SHOULD BE THE TEMPLATE:** this is RTL every target links,
  so the change must be a no-op off-ESP — **byte-identical x86-64 binary before and after** (nested
  records, dynarray of strings, variant, whole-record assignment, `Finalize`; still runs) — **AND it
  demonstrated the edit is not vacuous: the same change moved three ESP fixtures from `compiler error`
  to `ok`.** *No change where none should occur, real change where it should.* **Identity alone would
  have been a guard that cannot fail**, and it said so in those words unprompted.
- **The fixture asserts VALUES, not compilation** — `test_esp_bare_managed.pas` boots on esp32c3 and
  esp32s3 and matches its oracle byte-for-byte. **A compile-only row cannot tell a working walk from
  one that compiles and then corrupts a refcount**, which is the only failure mode that matters for
  routines whose whole job is refcount bookkeeping.
- **THIRD INSTRUMENT FAILURE OF THE DAY, inside a scanner it wrote: a file that documents its own
  syntax defeats the parser you write for it.** `builtinheap.pas` comments QUOTE directives because
  they explain the guards. An awk pattern escaping `$` matched **nothing** in a span containing two —
  mawk does not take that escape and **fails by ANSWERING**, same family as its missing `strtonum`
  this morning. Then a correct `^`-anchored Python regex applied to `line.strip()`: **the strip
  promotes an INDENTED quotation inside a comment to a column-0 directive**, so the depth counter
  incremented on prose and never found the real `endif`. ***"The strip looked like robustness.
  Whitespace was carrying the entire signal."***
- **Did NOT lift `PXX_ALLOW_FULL_SUITE`** — new rows are self-contained, ran them verbatim. The escape
  is for when you need it, not for when a change feels shared.
- **MY ANSWER ON THE VARIANT SPAN — do not take it as it stands, on its own finding.** It has just
  proved compile-only cannot distinguish a working walk from a corrupting one, and the variant span is
  **bigger, float-entangled, and has NO runtime oracle on bare** the way records now do. Taking it
  would ship the larger blast radius with the weaker instrument one step after proving why that fails.
  **Build the bare variant oracle first, or stop — and this is a clean place to stop.**

## ~19:30 — the all-clear is only as good as what the scan ENUMERATED. And I fixed the marker summary again.

**8e interrogated a CLEAN SCAN — the result nobody interrogates — and then measured its own question
rather than asserting it from the depth logic.** The query that produces a correct all-clear is
**lexical containment, not adjacency and not a number: does any `try` CONTAIN another `try` that guards
an import, at any distance, through any intervening construct?**

```python
try:
    x = 1
    if x == 1:              # an ordinary block BETWEEN the two tries
        try:
            import math
        except ImportError:
            from . import dead as impl
        else:
            from . import live as impl
except ValueError:
    pass
```
**Both halves fire on the pin — dead arm RAN, wrong module bound — with only an `if` in between**,
because the pre-scan compares INDENT depth and a non-try block pushes the guard deeper just as
effectively as a try does. **A scan for `try:` directly inside `try:` answers ZERO on that block.**

- **c6's TSP scan answers it correctly and is the template: an AST pass counting enclosing `try` NODES
  per import** (ancestor counting, so intervening constructs do not matter) — **509 at depth 0, 7 at
  depth 1, ZERO at 2+** over `tsp/` and `tests/`. **And c6 made it a STANDING TEST**
  (`tests/test_pxx_shape.py`, `4fccfbe`): reads sources with `ast` so it imports nothing, covers the
  SDL2/GL and dataset modules, asserts the seam's shape too, **and was verified to FAIL on planted
  counter-examples rather than passing vacuously.** Both failure modes are silent under CPython, so
  nothing else would catch a regression — **a note in a README cannot, a test can.** Asked c6 to run

## ~20:10 — both all-clears now rest on the STRUCTURAL predicate, and both apps have a self-verifying test.

**7a's scan was the structural reading — no re-run needed, all-clear stands.** An `ast.NodeVisitor`
incrementing on `visit_Try`, decrementing after `generic_visit`, recording the counter at every
`Import`/`ImportFrom`: **enclosing try NODES, blind to what sits between**, so no intervening
`if`/`def`/`for`/`class`/`with` can hide the nesting. **8e's block is a test row there now and reads
depth 2 for all three imports.** 8e's question closed a real gap in the OTHER application's method and
cost one exchange.

- **7a states its one weak spot rather than letting it stand, and disposes of it correctly:**
  `platform/_pxx.py` cannot be parsed by CPython's `ast`, so it got a **textual** scan — the weaker
  reading — **airtight only because the file contains no `try:` at all**, and **that is now ASSERTED
  rather than remembered.** Turning *"airtight because of something I checked once"* into a guard is
  the step almost everyone skips.
- **Both applications now hold a SELF-VERIFYING shape test** (c6 `4fccfbe`, 7a `fdee787`): reads
  sources with `ast` so it imports nothing, asserts no import at try-depth ≥ 2 and the seam's shape —
  and **on every run copies the package to a temp dir, plants five defects and requires the check to
  FAIL** (nested guard with an `if` between; nested guard behind a `def`; seam flattened; an arm
  forgetting `_backend_name`; the unparseable file gaining a try). 11 tests, 2.6 s.
  **THE CLAUSE THAT MATTERS IS "EACH CAUGHT BY THE RIGHT CHECK": a planted-defect harness where ANY
  assertion firing counts as a pass can hide that one specific check is dead.** That is the
  positive-control rule applied per-CHECK rather than per-harness, and it is why five are planted
  rather than one.
- **7a's thanks to 8e, with the sentence it asked be kept:** *8e's self-correction about putting the
  `def` AFTER the outer try is exactly the trap that harness exists to catch — 7a's own first planted
  case could have been wrong the same way and it would not have known.* **A planted defect that is not
  actually the defect passes the harness and certifies it** — 8e's malformed-probe finding arriving
  inside the tool built to prevent the thing 8e found.
- **Both failure modes are silent under CPython, so nothing else would have caught a regression here.**
  A README note cannot; these can. Nothing in this thread needs the owner.

## ~20:30 — caught a duplicate before it landed, and the `def` gap is half of Python's only `#ifdef`.

**8e filed `bug-n-a-def-inside-any-compound-statement-is-never-registered` locally (p60) — NOT pushed,
tier still running. THE CAUSE IS ALREADY ON ORIGIN**:
`bug-n-a-def-inside-a-taken-branch-does-not-rebind-the-name`, track N, **p45**, unowned, whose summary
already names the mechanism — ***"`PyRegisterDefShells` only walks module-level defs at DEPTH 0, so a
def inside a branch never gets one"*** — and records that it was split out of a module-rebinding
ticket when that one was fixed. **Same defect, independently measured, and it confirms 8e's correction
to itself: NOT try-specific, a depth-0 walk.** Told it to fold its evidence in rather than land a
second slug: **two slugs for one cause is how a backlog reaches 5035.**

**WHAT 8e HAS THAT THE EXISTING TICKET CANNOT SAY, and it is the more valuable half.** That ticket's
example is `if True:` with a synthetic rebinding — reads as an edge case, priced p45. **8e's is the
standard pure-Python fallback, and it is the direct companion to the idiom the owner ruled BY DESIGN
three hours ago:**

```python
try:
    from somewhere import isqrt
except ImportError:
    def isqrt(n): ...        # the ordinary fallback
```

CPython prints 4; pxx says `unresolved forward: isqrt`. **So pxx supports the conditional IMPORT and
not the conditional DEFINITION — half of Python's only `#ifdef`.**

**AND THE ASYMMETRY IS WHAT SHOULD SET THE PRIO: it passes exactly when the fallback is UNUSED and
fails exactly when it is TAKEN.** Guard on `math` — a module we HAVE — and it compiles, because the
guard resolves, the handler is dead, and the `def` in it is skipped without ever being registered.
**Under pxx the failing case is the COMMON one, since the reason to write a fallback is a module we may
lack.** That is also **why no fixture ever caught it: an author reaching for this idiom naturally
guards on something present.**
One row pinned as **NOT a divergence**: `def` inside `except ValueError:` fails under CPython too
(`NameError`, handler never runs) — pinned deliberately so nobody "fixes" it into printing 7.

- **Grep relayed to 7a and c6: `except ImportError:` with a `def` in the handler. Their new shape tests
  will NOT catch it** — different defect from the one those assert, and both packages are exactly the
  kind of code that writes it.
- **8e's sharpening of the harness clause, banked ITS way: "each caught by the RIGHT check" outranks
  the count of five.** A planted-defect harness that passes when ANY assertion fires **cannot detect a
  DEAD check — the very failure it exists to prevent — so it certifies itself. Five plants with loose
  attribution is weaker than two with strict attribution.**
- **8e asked that 7a's version of the malformed-probe finding circulate instead of its own, and it is
  right:** its bad probe cost minutes in a scratch file; **a plant that is not actually the defect gets
  the harness a green and is then TRUSTED FOREVER** — the same error promoted into permanent
  infrastructure, where nothing downstream can notice.

## ~11:16 — folded, and the reason goes past tidiness: A DUPLICATE CAN LET THE ORIGINAL CLOSE ON AN
## ARGUMENT THE DUPLICATE REFUTES.

**8e deleted its neighbour and folded the evidence into
`bug-n-a-def-inside-a-taken-branch-does-not-rebind-the-name`, re-ranked 45 -> 65. STILL LOCAL — I
checked the ref: origin holds one `def-inside` ticket and it reads `prio: 45`.** Correct behaviour
(mid-tier, not committing), and worth saying out loud because today's recurring theme is exactly this
gap: **"folded" is not "landed."** The neighbour never reached origin, so nothing needs unpicking.

**THE FINDING IS NOT THE RE-RANK.** That ticket reasons toward closing as *"conditional bindings are
not tracked, by design, in both directions"*, citing upward compatibility — a `f = ...` under a branch
that never runs must not displace a def. **Sound for a REBIND, and it has nothing to bite on for the
new case: a conditional def with no prior definition is the ONLY definition, there is nothing to
displace, CPython makes it, and pxx refuses the program.** The symmetry escape does not reach the
second observable.

**SO THE REASON FOLDING WAS RIGHT IS A MECHANISM, NOT AN AESTHETIC: had the neighbour landed, that
ticket would have KEPT ITS BY-DESIGN ESCAPE INTACT AND COULD HAVE BEEN CLOSED ON IT — with the
refuting case in a different slug, unread by whoever closed it.** The evidence had to sit in the same
file as the argument it defeats. **Anywhere a ticket carries a proposed by-design resolution, a sibling
filed elsewhere is how that resolution survives its own counterexample.** Not stated anywhere in this
tree; banked, one instance, on the owner's candidate list.

- **Mechanism confirmed rather than assumed:** `PyRegisterDefShells` tests
  `(depth = 0) and (Tokens[i].Kind = tkFunction)` — nothing at depth > 0 visited, whether or not a name
  already exists. **One cause, two observables**, which is precisely what makes the by-design escape
  only half-applicable.
- **A SECOND GATE CLAUSE ADDED, and it is the operative part: the existing gate (`g()` answers 2) does
  not cover the REFUSAL case at all**, so a fix satisfying the old gate could ship with the `isqrt`
  shape still refused. **A gate that cannot see the second observable closes the ticket early.**
- **8e's version of how it found the duplicate is sharper than mine and it chose the worse-looking
  one.** I framed it as "already on origin"; it checked and found **the ticket is in its own
  25-commits-behind checkout too** — *"one `ls` of `backlog-nilpy` would have found it before I wrote a
  word"* — **proposing a mechanism without grepping for it**, a named rule here. Logbook, one line.
  It verified against the REF rather than its checkout **because it made the stale-tree mistake four
  hours ago and would not repeat it in the same session.**

never appears.

idiom.** Told 8e to **re-derive the 45 -> 65 if any of it rested on demo impact**, and to say in the

the absence arguably makes it *more* honest: a conformance defect ranked as one, with no demo lending
it borrowed weight. **A prio whose stated reason has been falsified is how a seat gets dispatched to
dead work**, and two seats handed us the falsification for free.

  predicate — a def with a compound statement between it and its enclosing scope — is the right one and
