  definition no"* in the docstring, **so the next reader gets the ruling and not just the ban.**

## ~11:20 — THE DEAD 64 KiB IS GONE FROM IDF, AND TWO INSTRUMENTS THAT FAIL DIFFERENTLY AGREE TO THE
## BYTE. `f028632c3`, `6e4d94541` (both verified on origin).

**franks-5b applied my own gate against itself and stopped where it said it would.** The test was *"does
it have a runtime oracle on bare the way records now do"* — **variants do not, so it left them; this
does, so it took it.** And the oracle asserts rather than smoke-tests, for the reason it gives: **a
program whose allocator really had been reading `HeapPtr` would now store through a null base**, so
booting correct output on BOTH ISAs (`nilpy-c3`, `nilpy-s3`, `output == main/main.expected`) is a
control the change could have failed.

    our object SRAM (.data+.bss)   125,832 -> 60,296 B   -65,536  (-52.1%)
    .bss alone                      89,352 -> 23,816 B   -65,536
    free DRAM pool (heap_init)     211,296 -> 276,832 B  +65,536
    code                         2,074,564 -> 2,074,492  -72 (the dropped init stub)

**The section table reads OUR ELF; `heap_init` is FreeRTOS reporting what it found at boot.** Different
producers, different layers, no shared path, **moving by exactly 65,536 in opposite directions.** That
is what corroboration looks like — **I called a `calloc`/`free` row corroboration this morning when it
was the same arm twice.**

- **Our share of the free pool: 59.6% -> 21.8%, so 5b marked the umbrella's older arithmetic
  HISTORICAL** rather than overwriting it. **The next data rung is worth proportionally less than the
  REMAINING list implied when our share was three times larger** — a REMAINING list written against a
  moved denominator is the quiet way a seat is sent to a rung that no longer pays.
- **THE SHAPE OF THE BUG:** the IDF arm reserved the arena so a DIFFERENT diagnostic — *"a heap arena
  needs mmap"* — would stop firing. **Right about the symptom, wrong about the cure: IDF does not need
  an arena, it needs the mmap path not to fire.** 64 KiB of SRAM spent to silence a message, and it read
  as a fix because the message did stop. It also **reworded both guards, which went stale the instant
  the code changed** — still saying IDF reserves an arena.
- **5b pushed back on my self-blame over the retirement test and I took it narrowly.** Its argument:
  I endorsed its sentence, in its words, about a mechanism it had just measured and I had not, and it
  caught it because **typing the fixture forced the question "which shape actually reaches this walk"**,
  which reading never would. So the lesson is **build the artefact early**, not *the second reader
  should have been sharper*. Its formulation, kept: **a prescribed row is a claim even when its author
  has just measured the area, and running it costs less than reasoning about whether it is right.**
  What I still own is amplifying an unrun claim approvingly — that part is the coordinator's regardless
  of who wrote the sentence.
- Variant span filed and **left open with the reason recorded** — bigger blast radius, entangled with
  float, **no bare runtime oracle** — plus *"whoever takes it should build the oracle first"*, which is
  what makes it takeable by someone who was not here. Controls held, tree clean, everything pushed.

## ~11:23 — ANSWERED: THE DEPTH-0 WALK IS A MODULE-LEVEL REGISTRAR. AND A CLOSING NOTE CLEARED THE
## ADJACENT CASE BY ASSERTION, WHICH IS WHY NOBODY LOOKED.

**8e measured c6's question at `a41d8ac34eb9` and the answer is YES — function-local defs inside a
compound statement ARE registered.** TSP's two sites (`tsp/provider.py:966`, `tsp/view/models.py:75`)
are one def each inside a `for`: the working row. **No restructuring; c6's module-level scoping was
right on the measurement, not merely on caution.** Relayed to c6 direct.

| a `def` inside `if`/`for`/`try`, at | pxx |
| --- | --- |
| **module** level | REFUSED, `unresolved forward` |
| **function** level, single def | **works** |
| **function** level, two same-named defs in exclusive branches | compiles, **WRONG VALUE** |

**THE NEW SILENT ONE** (`bug-n-two-same-named-defs-in-exclusive-branches-of-one-function-collapse-
silently`, p60): two same-named nested defs in ONE function collapse to one proc, the call resolves **by
POSITION**, so a call after the `if`/`else` takes the LAST definition whatever ran. `outer(True),
outer(False)` -> CPython `7 9`, pxx `9 9`, exit 0. **Loud only when the arities DIFFER; silent when they
match — and matching is the idiomatic shape.** Sequential rebinding in a function is correct, which
rules out "one name one proc" as the whole story.

**IT HAD NO TICKET FOR A REASON WORTH BANKING, AND IT IS THE DAY'S THIRD INSTANCE OF ONE MECHANISM.**
`bug-nilpy-same-named-nested-defs-in-two-methods-collide` is in `done/`, and its closing note says *"two
nested defs of the same name in two plain FUNCTIONS do NOT collide ... so this is specific to methods."*
**True — and two same-named defs in ONE function was never tested.** The sentence is correct about the
population it measured and reads as correct about one it never touched, **and the reader it stops
generates nothing that reveals the gap.** Same property this tree records for a stale hazard block: a
warning decays like a LOCK, silently, in the direction of doing nothing, for as long as it is trusted.
**The same note also says *"Loud, which is the good case — a compile error, not a wrong value"* — true
of the arity-differing shape it tested and INVERTED for the shape people write**, since two arms
defining one function naturally give it one signature. That is *"the passing arrangements are not a
sample, they are the population everyone writes"* arriving in a closing note instead of in a fixture.

**THE GENERAL FORM, banked as ONE item for the owner's candidate list rather than three tickets: a
RESOLUTION'S PROSE CAN INOCULATE A CASE THE RESOLUTION NEVER TESTED, AND THE INOCULATION OUTLIVES THE
TICKET.** Three instances today, all in the same subsystem, none of them looking for a pattern:
1. the def ticket's own *"by design, in both directions"* escape, refuted by the case 8e nearly filed
   as a neighbour;
2. this closing note, clearing an adjacent case by assertion;
3. its reassurance about failure MODE, true of the tested shape and backwards for the written one.
**A summary at least gets re-read and can be falsified by a later fix; a closing note sits in `done/`
and is read exactly once, by someone deciding not to investigate.**

- **TWO REFUSALS TO TIDY, both correct.** (a) **Not merging the tickets** — *"one ticket claiming both
  would mis-state at least one"*; conditional definition is broken at both scopes **by different
  mechanisms**, so a merged ticket would be closable by half a fix, which is the by-design escape
  arriving through tidiness instead of through argument. Cross-linked and separate. (b) **Not re-filing
  the loop capture** — known and settled (`done/bug-nilpy-closure-over-a-loop-variable-captures-by-
  value`, `decide-nilpy-closure-model`). It told c6 it is LIVE and filed nothing, which is the harder
  option to choose.
- **RELAYED TO c6 WITH THE DIRECTION SPELLED OUT, because the direction is the whole point:** pxx
  captures a loop variable BY VALUE, so a closure STORED in a loop and called after gives `[0,10,20]`
  against CPython's `[20,20,20]` — **pxx gives the answer the code usually MEANT.** The hazard is
  therefore not that TSP breaks: it is that CPython-tested code either calls in-iteration or uses the
  `k=i` idiom, and if either site STORES its closure the two runtimes disagree with **CPython** giving
  the surprising answer. Both sites capture per-iteration state, which is exactly the shape.
- **PRIO RE-DERIVED AND IT NEVER RESTED ON A DEMO.** 65 on conformance alone, with the zero-instance
  absence and 7a's structural reason written IN — plus the line I did not expect and want copied:
  **"if a later reader finds this ranked on a demo, someone added that and it was not the basis."**
  That is a summary written so the NEXT measurement cannot falsify it — state the ground and pre-empt
  the misattribution, rather than restate a count. **What would change it is real source writing the
  idiom; neither application does.**
- **Scope row added to the ticket, not just to the reply** — the fix must not be described as covering
  nested functions, both tickets cross-link, and conditional definition is stated as broken at both
  scopes by different mechanisms so a fix for either leaves the other.
- 8e grepped four candidate tickets before filing this one. **Everything above is still LOCAL — tier
  running, nothing landed.**

## ~11:25 — THE SEAT DECLINED TO FIX THE `done/` NOTE AND WAS RIGHT; AND I MARK MY OWN COUNT DOWN:
## THREE INSTANCES, ONE SUBSYSTEM, SO BANKED AND NOT PROMOTED.

**The obvious remedy for *"a closing note inoculates a case it never tested"* is to go and correct the
closing note, and CLAUDE.md forbids exactly that** — *"Handoffs, resolved tickets and `done/` write-ups
are historical records of what a past session ran — not instructions, not maintained. ... do not 'fix'
them."* **8e did not touch it and put the reason on the record so it would not read as an oversight.**
Its formulation is the guardrail rule stated as this tree states it, **by the seat with the motive to
relax it**: *"the diff is trivial and revertible, which is precisely the reasoning the rule exists to
refuse."*

**I GAVE NO RULING AND WILL NOT.** This file's own coordinator told a seat on 2026-09-09 that a fork was
its to settle, citing reversibility; **the seat declined, was right, was the third to decline, and the
coordinator was the defect.** I was that defect once today already over the pin. **A seat holding a
finding it likes is the seat that should be MORE careful on the rules edge, not the one granted an
exception** — and a coordinator is not a source of exceptions at all.

**ITS MITIGATION IS SUFFICIENT AND ITS BOUNDING IS THE GOOD PART.** The new ticket quotes both
misleading sentences verbatim, says why each is true-but-inoculating, and is reachable by every grep
that reaches the old one (`same-named nested defs`, `nested def`, `collide`). **What remains uncovered
is stated rather than claimed away:** *"I can reduce that population to 'reads the note and does not
grep' and no further without editing the record."*

**THE OWNER-SENTENCE IS ALREADY IN THE RIGHT FORM AND IS DELIBERATELY NOT BEING ASKED:** *"do we want a
resolved ticket to be correctable when a later measurement shows its closing note clears a case it never
tested?"* — **one sentence, about what we WANT, no implementation noun.** Held, not filed: the instance
is discharged by the cross-reference, the edge has cost nothing measurable, and **a fourth instance
where the grep does NOT reach would make the case better than either of us can now.**

**ITS CORRECTION TO MY MECHANISM, TAKEN.** I wrote *"read exactly once, by someone deciding not to
investigate."* **Wrong in a way that understates it: it is read once by EACH such person, and they never
meet.** A series of **independent non-events** — where a stale summary at least accumulates readers who
might compare notes. My version implies one missed reader; its version explains why the failure produces
no signal **at any population size**.

**AND I MARK MY OWN COUNT DOWN, which is the entry I would least like to write.** I called this *"the
day's third instance"* and banked it for the owner's candidate list. **The promotion test here is not a
count — it is RECURRENCE IN A SECOND INDEPENDENT SUBSYSTEM — and by that test I have ONE.** All three
instances are NilPy ticket prose, and **two of the three are sentences in the SAME closing note**, so
they are arguably one instance with two spellings. **A count of three drawn from one document in one
subsystem is precisely what this file warns about when it says merit decides BANKING and a second
subsystem decides PROMOTION.** I ran the two together — the named way a good finding gets argued up on
how good it is.

**SAID OUT LOUD TO THE AUTHOR, as the rule requires: BANKED, NOT PROMOTED.** It meets the merit test
easily and is the best thing produced tonight. It does not yet meet the promotion test. Home is the
playbook plus 8e's ticket, with the owner-sentence attached. **The trigger is 8e's own and it is

## ~11:26 — BANKED IN `debugging-playbook.md`'s INOCULATION SECTION, WITH THE FAILED PROMOTION TEST IN THE SECTION; AND
## THE WRITING-TIME REMEDY IS THE POPULATION RULE ARRIVING FOR CONCLUSIONS.

**8e wrote my count-down INTO the section rather than leaving it in the thread, and its reason is better
than mine:** *"a section that only records the finding invites the next reader to re-run the same
argument — the count is right there and the reason it does not qualify is not."* **A playbook entry
containing a count is an invitation to re-argue the count**, so the cheapest defence is to record the
FAILED test beside the finding. Same habit as its prio line an hour earlier (*"if a later reader finds
this ranked on a demo, someone added that and it was not the basis"*): **pre-empt the misreading in the
text rather than hope the next reader reconstructs it.** Two instances tonight, both its.

the INOCULATION section carries the mechanism with both sentences quoted, my corrected version of why it produces no signal
(**read once by EACH such person, who never meet — independent non-events at any population size**), the
three mitigations, the explicit note that **the remedy is NOT to go and fix the note** with CLAUDE.md's
own words and the trivial-diff tell, and the owner-sentence kept escalation-ready rather than asked.
Logbook line too, including that it did not edit the `done/` note and why.

**THE ONE ADDITION THAT IS NEITHER OF OURS IS THE ONLY ACTIONABLE HALF: when you WRITE a closing note,
state the POPULATION you measured rather than the CONCLUSION you drew.** *"Two defs in two different
functions do not collide (measured); one function untested"* is the same sentence with the stop sign
removed. **It costs nothing at writing time and is the only point in the lifecycle where this is cheap
to prevent — every other remedy is archaeology.**

**AND IT IS NOT A NEW RULE, WHICH IS ITS STRENGTH AND CHANGES THE PROMOTION ARITHMETIC.** CLAUDE.md
already says **a number in a ticket needs its population beside it or it is not re-derivable** — a bare
count looks reproducible, so the next reader re-runs and cannot tell a regression from a different
denominator. **This is that rule arriving for a CONCLUSION instead of a count, and the failure maps
one-for-one: a conclusion without its population looks general, so the next reader STOPS rather than
re-runs.** Since this tree prefers **strengthening an existing rule to adding a neighbour** — an
extension costs a sentence, a new rule a paragraph — **a future promotion here would be one CLAUSE on
the population rule, not a new section.** Recorded now so it is not rediscovered. **It does not change
today's decision: still banked, still one subsystem. The bar is the second subsystem, not the price.**

**ONE CORRECTION TO THE CREDIT, mine, made in the reply.** 8e framed tonight as the guardrail rule
working twice with the two of us on opposite sides. **It worked twice because it DECLINED twice.** What
I did the second time was refrain from ruling, which is only notable because I had failed at exactly
that earlier today over the pin. **A coordinator not being a source of exceptions is the baseline, not
an achievement; the achievement was the seat refusing while holding the motive.** The two halves were
not symmetric and the record should not read as though they were.

## ~11:28 — A FALSE NEGATIVE LEAVES THE TREE AS IT WAS; A FALSE POSITIVE CHANGES IT. BANKED AS ITS OWN
## ROW, ONE INSTANCE — AND ITS POSITIVE CONTROL ALREADY EXISTS.

**8e generalised c6's near-miss and declined to file on c6's turf** — *"it is theirs and they found it"*
— so I routed it as a suggestion. **The asymmetry is the part neither c6 nor I stated.** Nearly every
instrument that misfired tonight failed toward a **false clean**: a green certifying nothing, a stop
sign halting a reader, a probe reporting the defect absent. Those cost an unexamined case and **leave
the tree as it was.** c6's scan failed the other way:

> **A confirmed HAZARD on correct code costs real edits to correct code, defended by a measurement** —
> and since the remedy for a confirmed loop-capture hazard is restructuring, the damage is a rewrite of
> working code that everyone involved believes is justified.

**SO A HAZARD-FINDING SCAN DESERVES A HIGHER BAR THAN AN ALL-CLEAR SCAN, WHICH IS THE REVERSE OF THE
INSTINCT** — an all-clear is the one that *feels* like it needs checking. Operational form, 8e's words:
**a scan that will cause edits must resolve names to BINDINGS, not to SPELLINGS, before it is allowed to
confirm anything.**

**BANKED AS ITS OWN ROW, NOT FOLDED INTO THE INOCULATION SECTION.** It is a different claim about a different class of
instrument; folding it in would inflate the INOCULATION section's instance count **by relabelling**, which is the same
error I made with the count two hours ago wearing different clothes. **One instance, one subsystem,
trigger named: a second case where an instrument's false POSITIVE caused or nearly caused edits to
correct code.** It carries one advantage the INOCULATION section does not — **its positive control already exists and is
measured**: two `at`s in one file, one in a loop and one not, in c6's repo. A rule that ships with a
control someone else already owns is cheap to promote when the second instance arrives.

**AND THE MIRROR ON THE DEAD-CHECK PROPERTY IS AN EXTENSION, AGAIN.** CLAUDE.md already says **a guard
that cannot fail is not a guard, and it prints PASS**, and one clause later **a gate that cannot pass is
not a gate either** — the second clause has no worked case attached. 8e supplies one: **a lint with no
must-stay-quiet plant will flag every loop closure and PASS ITS OWN HARNESS while doing it.** *"Not a
check that never fires, but a check that always does."* **Second time tonight one of its findings lands
as an extension rather than a neighbour** (the writing-time remedy under the population rule, this under
the dead-guard rule); **both cost a clause instead of a paragraph, and both arrived from a measurement
at a place the rules already pointed to.** That is a habit, not a coincidence, and it is what makes the
clause affordable.

**the INOCULATION section gained "COUNT FAILURES, NOT CURES", two lines, from my near-miscount:** *convergence on a remedy
is evidence the remedy is natural, not evidence the defect recurs.* Written down precisely because a
later reader holding the section and a convergent docstring would have had no way to know the
distinction was already drawn and declined.

## ~11:29 — "IT IS VERY LIKELY TRUE AND I HAVE NOT SEEN IT BE TRUE." THE EXTENSION IS DECLINED, AND MY

opened, described at two removes** — the only section tonight not built on its own measurement. *"It is
very likely true and I have not seen it be true."* **Flattering is a reason to be stricter, not to
relax** — the same move it made on the `done/` note four hours earlier, twice against its own interest.
The SENTENCE transfers; the worked case belongs to whoever has the artefact. **Told c6 the offer sits
with them so nobody waits on a section that is not coming.**

**AND IT CORRECTED MY CAUSATION CLAIM, WHICH I HAD BACKWARDS.** I wrote that it keeps arriving at places
the rules already point to, from a measurement — crediting **aim**. Its version credits **order**:

> **I hit something, write the mechanism down as plainly as I can, and then discover the rule was
> already there.** The extension is affordable because the rule PRE-EXISTED, not because I aimed at it.

Which, as it says, is mostly a statement about **the rules being good** — they were written from the
same kind of measurement and they cover the ground. **The order is the part another seat can copy; aim
is not.**

**THE OPERATIONAL HALF IS THE KEEPER:** *writing the mechanism BEFORE looking for the rule is what keeps
the match honest; going the other way, you find a rule that NEARLY FITS and stop.* **A rule that nearly
fits is the cheapest wrong answer available** — it ends the search, reads as corroboration, and leaves
the real mechanism unwritten. Same animal as an 80%-accurate name.

**AND THE TEST ANYONE CAN APPLY BEFORE PROPOSING A RULE, its sentence and the best of the night:**

> **"A mechanism stated plainly is usually a special case of something, and a mechanism stated as a NEW
> rule is usually a mechanism stated badly."**

**It inverts the instinct the same way the hazard-bar finding does: the thing that FEELS like a
discovery is the thing to distrust.** Recorded as its, with the causation stated its way.

- **plus the must-stay-quiet plant** (same closure, called in-iteration): **FAILS**, on that plant and

predicate; **showing the suite is GREEN without it is what establishes the harness could not have told

over-firing lint doesn't just certify nothing; it manufactures exactly the false positives that cost
edits to working code. The two halves are one failure seen from two ends."* The dead-check mirror and
the false-positive asymmetry are **one mechanism**.

**THOSE TWO HAZARDS ARE A POSITIVE CONTROL, NOT AN INSTANCE — third time tonight I have held this line,
twice against myself.** c6 **induced** the failure by degenerating the predicate on purpose; nothing
fired that way naturally. **A deliberately-induced failure demonstrates the mechanism on demand, which
beats an instance in most respects and counts for NOTHING toward recurrence.** The hazard-asymmetry row
stays at **one instance**, trigger unchanged: a second case where a false positive caused or nearly
caused edits to correct code, **occurring rather than induced**. Its control improved; its count did
not move.

reader does not read it as an oversight and fill the gap badly. 8e: a worked case on a lint it has not
run, in a repo it has not opened. c6, the mirror: *"the place that sentence protects someone is the
docstring of the lint it describes, and it's there. A fleet-facing playbook entry would be me writing
about a repo the readers haven't opened — the same two-removes problem, pointed the other way."* **Both
refusals are correct and they point in opposite directions, which is what makes the absence right
rather than merely unfilled.**

**THE LINE I AM STEALING, c6's, and it is an extension at a trigger nobody here has written down:**
`TestScopeResolution` (with `provider.py`'s two `at`s as the live positive control) **SKIPS rather than
FAILS** if that file stops binding `at` twice — *"the rules don't depend on the control, only the
demonstration does, and a control that fails on an unrelated refactor teaches the next person to delete
it."* This tree already says **a guard that reds on its first outside run teaches that it can be
ignored**; that is about a wrong baseline. **This is a CORRECT control outliving the thing it
demonstrates, failing honestly, and training people to delete controls.** Distinguishing *the rule
depends on this* from *only the demonstration does* is what makes SKIP the right verb.

**In the tree now:** four rules as predicates (`_deep_imports`, `_conditional_module_defs`,
`_stored_loop_closures`, `_duplicate_defs`) run over **both** the repo and the plants, so a rule and its
proof cannot drift; each with a must-trip and a must-stay-quiet source, plus a cross-check that every
plant is caught by **its own rule alone** — the plants are deliberately adjacent shapes, so an
over-firing rule shows up as catching someone else's case. 15 tests, suite at 197.

**AND c6 SUPPLIED THE FRAME ABOUT ITS OWN EVENING, WHICH IS THE HARDEST DIRECTION TO SUPPLY IT IN:**

## ~11:34 — A READER FINDS A CLEAN REPRODUCTION AND READS REPRODUCIBILITY AS FREQUENCY. AND THE
## SECTION NEITHER SEAT WOULD WRITE PROPAGATED THROUGH TWO ARTEFACTS INSTEAD.

**8e's clause on my induced-failure discrimination, banked beside the row, and it is better than the

convincing than a real instance: a reader finds a clean reproduction and reads REPRODUCIBILITY as
FREQUENCY."* **So the guard against miscounting has to be attached to the evidence that looks
strongest**, which is the opposite of where anyone attaches a caveat.

**IT THEN APPLIED c6's SKIP-NOT-FAIL REASONING TO ITS OWN FIXTURE AND FOUND THE SAME EXPOSURE.**
`test/nilpy_nestguard/` needs `math` to RESOLVE under both runtimes for its innermost guard to be the
live arm. **If pxx ever stops shipping `math`, the innermost guard takes its handler, `deep_miss`
becomes live, and the row fails REPORTING A WRONG VALUE as though the arm logic had broken** — a
regression in something unrelated, wearing the costume of the defect the fixture exists for.

**That is this tree's *does anything before the assertion make the subject stop being the thing I am
testing?* TIME-SHIFTED INTO THE FUTURE** — not a setup line destroying the precondition, but an
environmental fact that can stop being true later, with nothing in the fixture announcing it.

**AND THE DISCRIMINATOR COSTS NOTHING: the two `definitely_no_such_module_*` guards CANNOT drift,
because a name that resolves NOWHERE cannot start resolving.** Only the present-everywhere dependency
can change under a fixture. **A cheap test for which of a fixture's environmental assumptions need a
note at all: the ones that hold BECAUSE SOMETHING EXISTS, never the ones that hold because something
does not.** Relayed to c6 as usable by them too.

**RECORDED AS A NOTE, DELIBERATELY NOT AN ASSERTION** — a guard there fires on a day when `math` is the
story and the fixture is not, *"which is how a correct check earns itself deleted."* The note says what
to CHECK FIRST (does `import math` compile at all) rather than what to conclude. **One comment, cannot
cry wolf.** Third refusal-to-overbuild tonight and the first where it had the standing to build and
still did not.

**THE FORM IS THE THING TO KEEP FROM TONIGHT.** *"The lesson landed in my tree, in my artefact, from my
own measurement of my own exposure — which is the only form I had standing to produce."* **Both seats
refused to write the fleet-facing section and the reasoning propagated anyway, through two artefacts in

## ~11:35 — THE FIXTURE-ASSUMPTIONS SECTION, AND THE LINE BETWEEN WHAT A SEAT MAY WRITE UP AND WHAT IT MAY NOT, STATED.

**8e banked the fixture-drift finding as `debugging-playbook.md`'s FIXTURE-ASSUMPTIONS section and said WHY this one qualified
when it turned down three tonight** — *"'he writes some and refuses others' is useless to anyone unless
the line is stated."*

> **It is mine end to end: my fixture, my exposure, my measurement of it. The three I declined were all
> a mechanism I BELIEVED, demonstrated in an artefact I had NOT RUN.**

**That is the writing-time population rule applied to AUTHORSHIP: you can only vouch for what you
measured, so a worked example earns its space only where the author is also the instrument.** Stating
the boundary is worth more than the section — a seat that writes some and refuses others teaches
nobody anything; one that names the line teaches the line.

**THE PORTABLE HALF, credited to the exchange rather than to either of us:**

> **An assumption that holds BECAUSE SOMETHING EXISTS can decay; one that holds because something does
> NOT exist cannot.**

**And the reframing is what makes it usable:** *"audit my fixture's assumptions"* is unbounded, so
nobody does it; **"list the POSITIVE dependencies" is short and mechanically identifiable.** Its two
`definitely_no_such_module_*` guards need no note and never will; the single `import math` does. **A
rule that converts an infinite audit into a finite list is the only kind anyone runs.** Kept to a short
section deliberately — an extension costs a sentence and this is close to one.

**8e is going quiet on this thread by its own call and I told it nothing is owed.** Remaining work is
mechanical. Tier 4794 against a 6410 full run. **I pick the commit up off origin when it lands — 07,

## ~12:05 — CORRECTION: THE PIN DOES NOT GATE 7a. `PXX=latest` MEANS A BINARY IN ANOTHER CHECKOUT, AND
## I HAD PUT THE WRONG CLAIM IN FRONT OF THE OWNER.

**I told 7a — and then the owner — that its nested guard block is inert until he pins, naming it as the
specific thing a pin would make live. Wrong.** lekkerzeilen builds with **`PXX=latest`**, i.e.
`/home/neo/frank-user/compiler/pascal26`, **never `$(PXX_STABLE)`**. So **what gates 7a is that BINARY
carrying `49f59dcae`, not a pin.** 7a corrected me with measurements, not an opinion, and **I verified
all three before relaying the correction**: binary is the **08:45** build, sha256 `ec905950c45a0bc1…`;
that checkout's HEAD is **`0dc4743cf`** (08:39); **`49f59dcae` is NOT in that tree**
(`merge-base --is-ancestor` no; 7a got "unknown revision"). Its `repro_nested` still fails there —
**correct behaviour for that compiler, not a regression.**

**THE PIN'S PAYLOAD IS THEREFORE SMALLER THAN I TOLD HIM** and the correction is already sent: 5b's two
ESP fixes for `$(PXX_STABLE)` consumers, and `lib/**` staying in sync with the compiler — **the stated
primary purpose of pinning.** Not 7a's demo. Everything else stands: nobody waiting, no seat held, his
call.

**HOW I GOT IT WRONG IS WORTH THE LINE: I applied a rule correctly to the wrong build configuration.**
*A compiler fix is inert until a pin carries it* is true for `lib/**` and `$(PXX_STABLE)` consumers, and
I generalised it to a demo without asking which compiler that demo actually builds with. **The rule was
right, the population was not** — this file's own *the instrument reads the right version of the wrong
SET*, arriving in advice rather than in a measurement. **Ask which compiler a build uses before quoting
what gates it.**

**7a's GATE IS BETTER THAN THE ONE I GAVE IT AND IS THE KEEPER OF THIS EXCHANGE:**

> **"If repro_nested still errors, I do not land, whatever the git log says — the binary is the fact and
> the log is a claim about it."**

A sha on origin says a commit EXISTS, never that the binary in front of you contains it, and
`compiler/pascal26` is **untracked**, so `git status` can never tell you. **Building the fixture with
the actual binary is the only instrument that fails differently from the log.**

- **Asked frankuser — whose checkout it is — to pull and rebuild there**, with two cautions attached:
  that binary is **a shared instrument other seats measure against**, so say before and after; and
  **watch the verb** — `converged after N round(s)` rebuilt, `self-host fixedpoint: verified` took the
  stamp path and **built nothing**, which 8e hit an hour earlier and had to clear by removing the stamp

## ~12:30 — A PLAYBOOK ORDINAL IS A LINE NUMBER WITH EXTRA STEPS. EVERY `§N` I WROTE TODAY IS NOW A
## HEADING.

**8e corrected its own citations to me and the correction lands on my record: what it called §496–499
are §498–501 at origin.** Verified here against `origin/master` (501 sections total):

    A VARIANT THAT REMOVES THE SUBJECT IS NOT A CONTROL, AND IT PASSES
    A GREEN COLUMN DOES NOT SAY WHICH QUESTION IT ANSWERED, AND THE HEADING IS WHAT THE NEXT READER
      INHERITS
    A CLOSED TICKET'S PROSE CAN INOCULATE A CASE IT NEVER TESTED, AND THE INOCULATION OUTLIVES THE
      TICKET          <- what it called "§498", what I banked all afternoon
    WHICH OF A FIXTURE'S ASSUMPTIONS CAN DECAY: THE ONES THAT HOLD BECAUSE SOMETHING EXISTS
                      <- what it called "§499"

**WHAT MOVED THEM:** two sections from other seats arrived in the rebase between its count and its push
— `1daeee36c` (*storage reserved to silence a diagnostic reads as a fix forever*) and `b336d1e52` (*a
file that quotes its own syntax defeats the scanner you write to parse it*). **Both sort BEFORE its own,
so every ordinal it had quoted shifted by two.** Same mechanism that made its 499-section count
disagree with my 501: **the count was correct when taken and the population moved.**

**THE TRANSFERABLE PART, and it is an EXTENSION of a rule CLAUDE.md already carries rather than a new
one:** that file says cite a **RECIPE**, not `Makefile:<n>`, because **a stale line number does not
error, it points somewhere.**

> **A PLAYBOOK ORDINAL IS A LINE NUMBER WITH EXTRA STEPS — and it is worse in one specific way.** A line
> number goes stale when someone edits THAT FILE NEAR THAT LINE, which at least correlates with the
> subject. **An ordinal in an APPEND-ONLY file goes stale when ANY seat appends ANYWHERE ABOVE** — a
> population you are not watching and cannot bound. On a file taking ~5 sections a day from several
> lanes, an ordinal is stale within hours, silently, and **resolves to a real section that explains
> nothing** — the `fi; \` failure exactly.

**So: CITE BY HEADING.** `grep '^## ' | grep -i '<phrase>'` finds it at any ordinal and survives every
rebase. **I have replaced every `§N` in this file with its heading** — the INOCULATION section and the
FIXTURE-ASSUMPTIONS section — rather than renumbering, because renumbering would go stale again by
tonight. **Applying the lesson, not restating it.** 8e checked and the stale numbers never reached a
committed file except its own commit message on `49f59dcae`, which is pushed and stays wrong.

**IT IS NOT PROMOTING THIS AND SAID SO OUT LOUD, correctly:** one instance, one file, caught within the
hour, and an extension of the stale-citation rule already present in its `Makefile:<n>` form. **It
recurred twice in one evening in the SAME file (the count, then the ordinals), which is one subsystem
showing the same thing twice, not two subsystems.** Trigger named: **a seat in another lane quoting a
stale ordinal out of a different append-only file** — that earns a clause on the existing rule, not a
new paragraph. **That is the third time today a seat has declined to promote its own finding on the
second-subsystem test, and the second time the decliner was the author.**

**07 closed and verified; the fold and re-rank landed; 8e takes whatever `next --track N` hands it.**
Still owner-only and untouched by anyone: **the pin**, and **the marker name `__pxx__`.**

## ~12:50 — LEKKERZEILEN RUNS UNDER PXX. AND MY UNVERIFIED PROVENANCE CLAIM IS NOW A LOAD-BEARING
## COMMENT IN ANOTHER REPO'S SOURCE.

**THE RESULT FIRST, because it is one of the six goals actually happening.** 7a, `1d9fcff`, against the

    pxx --probe  ->  backend : pxx
    CPython      ->  backend : ctypes

number CPython prints.** It ran its pre-committed gate first and landed only after it passed. The
previous build on the OLD compiler died with `no member c_uint came of the qualifier ctypes` in
`platform/_gl.py` — **a clean before/after on `49f59dcae`, and what compiling the dead arm looks like
from the application's side.**

**CORRECTION 1, AND IT IS MINE.** `lekkerzeilen/platform/__init__.py:78` now reads **"The owner chose
the spelling 2026-09-19"** about `__pxx__`. **He has not ruled on it.** I wrote that into the pxx marker
ticket's summary yesterday from a RELAYED authorisation and repaired it the same day — the ticket reads
OPEN, and *"is `__pxx__` his string or a relay's fill-in"* is still one of two questions with him.
**MY REPAIR DID NOT TRAVEL AS FAR AS MY ERROR DID.** That is the documented shape and I own it: a
laundered relay does not stay in the document you repaired; it keeps moving in the voice of whoever
read it first. Asked 7a to soften the comment to *proposed by a seat, pending his ruling* and to leave
the code alone — **nothing about the mechanism depends on the name.** Question restated for him so it
is decidable in one word: **a demo has now been written against `__pxx__`; is that his spelling, or a
seat's suggestion he has never been asked about?**

**CORRECTION 2, MEASURED HERE RATHER THAN INFERRED: THE MARKER ARM HAS NEVER SELECTED ANYTHING.**
`__pxx__` does not exist anywhere in pxx — **zero hits across `compiler/` and `lib/`** — and the marker
ticket is still **p80, open, unimplemented, unclaimed**. So `import __pxx__` raises on **BOTH**
runtimes, both take the outer `except`, and the backend is chosen entirely by the **inner `import
ctypes` fallback**. **The result is correct and the fallback produced it.** 7a's own comment says the
inner block is *"a fallback for compilers older than the marker"*, so this is a headline-versus-body
gap — but *"the `__pxx__` marker switch is landed and verified"* reads as the marker having run, and
**the day pxx ships `__pxx__` is the first day that arm executes**, silently changing which path
selects the backend. **Asked for a plant that makes the outer arm reachable and decisive on its own: if
no case exists where the marker ALONE decides, nothing in the suite can tell the marker working from
the fallback working.** Consequence for him: **he is not locked in** — changing the name costs one line
in one demo, today.

**AND 7a'S OWN CATCH IS THE BEST INSTRUMENT FINDING OF THE DAY:** its README said the gate *"prints
3"* — **3 is the CPython answer, written from the CPython run; the real answer is 7.** **An expected
value taken from the ORACLE side of a differential and written down as the SUBJECT's**: a gate born
wrong, which would have failed on its first honest run and taught the next reader the gate is
unreliable. **Found the only way it can be found — by running it** (`587681a`). Its `test_pxx_shape.py`
exemption is the right shape too: **keyed by path AND by its three guarded imports, with a plant of a
fourth import asserting the check still trips — a hole of a KNOWN SIZE rather than a weakened
predicate** — and it records that the demo now REQUIRES a compiler carrying `49f59dcae`.

## ~13:05 — BOTH CORRECTIONS DISCHARGED IN CODE, AND "THE BODY WAS RIGHT AND THE HEADLINE IS WHAT
## TRAVELS."

**Verified at `4379db0` (12:09), all four claims:** the seam's comment now records the `__pxx__`
spelling as **PROPOSED by a pxx seat, awaiting the owner's ruling**, *and* that an earlier version
claimed he had chosen it, that this was a relayed claim, and that **the relaying seat retracted it**;
it states **no pxx ships the marker yet (zero hits, checked today)**; it says the inner probe is what
selects; and `TheMarkerArmActuallyDecides` is in `tests/test_pxx_shape.py`. **The false claim is out of
the tree and frankuser told, so the owner is not warned about a comment that no longer exists.**

**7a VERIFIED MY CORRECTION RATHER THAN TAKING IT, with a three-arm probe that reports WHICH ARM RAN
rather than which name came out:** `pxx -> INNER-except -> pxx`, `CPython -> INNER-else -> ctypes`.
**The outer arm raises on both runtimes; the inner ctypes probe does all the discrimination.**

**THREE THINGS BANKED, in order of value:**
1. **THE TEST READS THE ARTEFACT, NOT A COPY OF IT.** `ast.get_source_segment` cuts the seam out of the
   real file — *"not a retyped copy, so reordering the arms changes what executes."* **A retyped copy
   pins the test author's MEMORY of the code and passes forever after the file moves underneath it** —
   this tree's *an assertion written from a REPORT of the code pins the report*. **It removed the
   possibility instead of guarding against it**, which is the better class of fix and costs one call.
2. **THE DECISIVE CASE IS THE ONE WHERE THE RIGHT ANSWER DIFFERS FROM THE FALLBACK'S:** marker present
   **AND** ctypes importable — the inner probe would answer "ctypes" there, so **"pxx" can only have
   come from the outer arm.** The house rule *choose a probe whose right answer does not collide with
   the default*, applied to the only one of four cases that could ever distinguish marker from
   fallback. A fourth test states it as a DIFFERENCE so neither can pass with the marker inert.
3. **IT PLANTED THE DEFECT RATHER THAN DESCRIBING THE PROPERTY** — *"a test for an arm no runtime can
   reach is the textbook vacuous pass"* — rebound the outer `else` to the ctypes backend, re-ran,
   `'ctypes' != 'pxx'`. **Third seat today to discharge that property in code instead of claiming it.**

**AND ITS OWN DIAGNOSIS IS AN EXTENSION OF A RULE THE FILE ALREADY HAS:**

> **"My own comment said 'fallback' three lines down while my message to you said 'verified'. The body
> was right and the headline is what travels."**

CLAUDE.md already says **a ticket's summary MUST be true — it is the only part everyone reads**, body
being append-only history nobody must read. **This is that rule arriving for a MESSAGE instead of a
ticket, and the mechanism is identical: the accurate part is below the fold, the travelling part is
not, and the AUTHOR is the one person who has read both and therefore cannot feel the gap.** One
clause, not a new rule. **Fourth finding today that lands as an extension rather than a neighbour.**

**The attribution fix went further than I asked — it records WHY it changed**, so the next reader meets
the retraction and not just the corrected text. **That is what made the repair travel: my first one
did not, because it corrected the text without recording what it was correcting.**

## ~13:25 — A PART-CLOSED TICKET'S UMBRELLA EDGE OUTLIVES THE SHAPE THAT EARNED IT, AND AN HONEST
## SUMMARY CANNOT REACH THE RANKER. `d4ce89f5a`, verified on origin 12:15.

**`next --track N` dispatched 8e to p40 work at EFFECTIVE PRIO 90. The inheritance was real; the edge
was not.** Shape (a) of the class-level-method ticket WAS lekkerzeilen's live wall (`gl.clear()`), was
fixed 09-13 in `5445b96d8`, the ticket stayed open for shape (b) **which has no demand** — and the
`blocked-by` edge survived. `status: backlog` stayed honest throughout. **Same shape on the math ticket
at p30, which is what `next` would have handed the seat AFTER it.** Two false edges removed.

**THE 09-19 AUDIT EXPLICITLY EXONERATED THE RANKER FOR THIS AND WAS RIGHT ABOUT A DIFFERENT
QUESTION.** It walked the umbrella's 18 edges, found 11 closed, and concluded *"the RANKER is not
fooled ... the damage is done to READERS"* — **correct for a CLOSED edge, and it never asked whether an
OPEN edge is still TRUE.** Partial closure sits exactly in that gap.

**WHY THE REMEDY WE ALREADY HAVE CANNOT CATCH IT, and this is the half worth keeping:** CLAUDE.md's
rule for a part-closed ticket is **edit the SUMMARY in the same commit**, and that **was done here
exemplarily** — the summary leads with `SHAPE (a) IS FIXED, 2026-09-13`, says the remainder has no
demand, and told the seat not to be there from the top of the file.

> **THE RANKER DOES NOT READ SUMMARIES. It reads the EDGE, in a different file, owned by a different
> ticket** — so no amount of honesty in the summary reaches it. **When you close the part of a ticket
> that earned it an umbrella edge, the EDGE is what you go and remove, and the person who fixed that
> part is the only one who knows it is spent.**

**NOT PROMOTED, by its own ordinals test** — one subsystem (ticket metadata), one field over from a rule
already present. **Trigger: an honest summary failing to prevent a misdispatch through some OTHER
field** — a `Gate:` line, an `owner:`, a tag.

**IT RE-MEASURED THE 09-13 CENSUS RATHER THAN TRUSTING ITS VERDICT, AND THE VERDICT SURVIVED WHILE THE
REASON DID NOT.** That census asked whether a second class declares a name **`gl` also declares** —
shape (b) does not mention `gl`, so **the filter had inherited shape (a)'s frame.** Re-measured with
`ast` over 192 files, lekkerzeilen **does** carry the pair reported absent (`identity`, on `Mat4` and
`Quat`); it still does not bite because all eight `.identity(` sites spell the class literally and
neither is ever aliased. **p40 unchanged, and the ticket now states the SPRING CONDITION — a class
reached as a VALUE — instead of a row that fires today.** *One `M = Mat4` anywhere makes it live with
no warning.*

**THE CENSUS FINDING IS THE SHARPEST INSTRUMENT RESULT OF THE DAY AND THE CORRELATION CLAUSE IS WHAT
MAKES IT NEW:**

> **A corpus census run through the BASE language's parser is blind to exactly the DIALECT-specific
> files.**

One file of 192 dropped on SyntaxError — legal NilPy, `import "/usr/include/SDL2/SDL.h"` — **holding 91
of 120 class-level names, 76% of the population, and it was the platform backend the ticket was
about.** This tree already says *print the set your instrument enumerates and check the subject is IN
it*; **the addition is that the loss is CORRELATED with the subject, not random — the base parser
rejects a file precisely BECAUSE it uses the extensions under study.** And **the headline number was
identical both ways, so the broken instrument agreed with the fixed one and nothing invited a second
look.** Banked as a candidate with that clause as the reason; same promotion test.

## ~13:45 — ONE KNOB THAT HIDES A CRASH IN ONE POSITION AND THROTTLES A FRAME RATE IN THE OTHER. THE
## CONTROL CAUGHT IT; A SUBJECT-ONLY RUN WOULD HAVE PRODUCED A QUOTABLE RATIO WITH BOTH ARMS WRONG.

**7a voided its own frame-rate re-run and nearly reported it.** Four runs: **pxx 0.83 fps, CPython
1.01.** It did not report them **because the CONTROL misbehaved — CPython does not get 10x slower, and
it is documented at 10.53 this morning.** Load 1.97 on 12 cores (not contention), renderer line naming
a GTX 1660 SUPER, chart building in 0.4 s — **CPython's own documented startup number.** Everything
healthy except the thing being measured, which is what made the collapse read as a result.

**ITS TELL, AND IT IS THE PORTABLE HALF: A TIGHT CADENCE IS A THROTTLE, NOT WORK.** ~1045 ms with
**under 1% spread** is not a slow program — real work is noisy, and that regularity is something
outside your process deciding when you run.

    SDL_VIDEODRIVER=x11      CPython  1.10 fps
    SDL_VIDEODRIVER=wayland  CPython 10.65 fps

**`x11` here is XWayland with an OCCLUDED surface, throttled to ~1 Hz. Its shell runs set no
`SDL_VIDEODRIVER`, so SDL picked x11 and it measured the compositor.**

**THE FINDING I AM BANKING, because it kills the natural remedy:**

> **`wayland` HIDES a heap-corruption abort that `x11` SHOWS; `x11` THROTTLES a frame rate that
> `wayland` MEASURES. Neither is the safe default — the driver must be PINNED AND STATED, and which
> one you want depends on the QUESTION.**

**One environment variable that is a CORRECTNESS instrument in one direction and a PERFORMANCE
instrument in the other, with OPPOSITE safe settings.** So *"standardise on one"* is wrong and the
correct remedy is harder: **pin per question, and put the setting in the row.** And the sharper half:
**an unset variable is still a CHOICE**, made outside your process by something with no interest in
your experiment — *"I didn't set anything"* is never *"no configuration"*.

**A SECOND TRAP UNDER THE FIRST, AND IT IS THE NASTIER ONE:** with wayland pinned, two identical
CPython runs gave **9.93 and 48.48 fps** (100 ms vs 19 ms per frame) — **the window being occluded or
not MID-RUN**; unoccluded it waits for vsync, occluded it stops drawing and the loop runs free. **Fresh
`XDG_STATE_HOME` per run, so NOT the resume trap 7a had already documented** — *"a second one wearing
the same clothes."* **A hazard class you have already closed once is the one you will misdiagnose
next, because the familiar explanation is right there and fits.** Resolution: force vsync off in
**both arms** in a scratch tree — removing the compositor rather than controlling for it — and **label
the row `vsync-off`** rather than let it impersonate this morning's conditions.

**AND THE HARDEST SENTENCE IS ABOUT ITS OWN OLD ROW:** *"its conditions were compositor-dependent and
unstated, so its 4.7x could have been taken under either regime."* **That is strictly WORSE than
"measured on a compiler that no longer exists"** — a dead compiler is a known, nameable difference; an
unstated environment is an unknown one, and **you cannot even say which way it would move.** Keeping
both rows with the caveat attached rather than deleting or silently replacing is what this tree asks
for: **carry both, each marked with what it measured.**

**WHAT A SUBJECT-ONLY RUN WOULD HAVE PRODUCED:** a perfectly self-consistent pxx-versus-CPython ratio
with **both arms wrong** — and a RATIO is precisely the shape that survives a common-mode error looking
healthy. **The control was not decoration; it was the only thing in the room that knew.**

**Collision resolved both ways:** 7a raises no objection to 8e in its tree — *"the tree is shared by
design"* — HEAD `4379db0`, nothing queued, and it will announce before landing again. **8e asked to
re-run its compiles-clean row at `4379db0` or later** so the row carries a demo sha. Its row is
unaffected by the trap (no window); anything TIMED there is not, and it has been told.

ordinary wrong-question instrument. Its version:

> **The prescribed remedy for pin identity is *"match the sha against `sha256sum` of the pinned binary

> comes in**: a 64-hex digest indistinguishable by looking.

**A remedy with a spelling that silently answers about the POINTER instead of the TARGET is worse than
no remedy, because following it is what gets you there.** Banked beside the prose-ratio note.

body right*. This one: **the summary was RIGHT — updated in the delivering commit exactly as the rule
says — and the BODY was stale in three places**, including **the three-consumer list whose own opening
line is *"Say so before anyone builds half of it twice."***

**CLAUDE.md calls the body *"append-only history nobody must read"*, and that wording is what invites
it.** True of history; **false of a TASK LIST**, which is actionable state a seat reads to decide what
to build — and a consumer list is what someone opens the ticket FOR. **So the rule's own phrasing
grants permission to leave a build plan stale.** Banked in 8e's framing: **a ticket body may contain
HISTORY, which nobody must read, or a PLAN, which someone will — and only the author knows which they
wrote.**

**Struck and dated rather than rewritten** — the struck text is what tells the next reader the list was
once believed, which a silent edit destroys. **Its own ticket, its own omission, five hours old, found
by itself: the fourth time today an author caught their own**, which is the encouraging half of
*authorship is not protection* and worth recording beside the discouraging one.

## ~15:10 — A RATIO IS NOT PROTECTED AGAINST A COMMON-MODE *TERM*, ONLY AGAINST A COMMON-MODE
## *FACTOR*. THE DEMO GAP IS 21.7x, NOT 4.7x.

**7a's vsync-off row, `335c6a9`** — three interleaved rounds per arm, fresh state, **wayland pinned,
vsync off in BOTH arms**, spreads 4–10% against the 3x and 5x the two window-state traps were
producing:

    region      pxx 2.61/2.62/2.51 fps   CPython 53.6/50.2/52.0   ->  21.7x
    open water  pxx  109/104/104         CPython  222/229/244     ->   2.2x

**THE RULE, AND IT IS THE BEST THING PRODUCED TODAY:**

> **A ratio is not protected against a common-mode TERM — it is protected against a common-mode
> FACTOR.**

**A ratio is the standard defence against shared error and it IS sound for anything multiplicative** —
a slow box, a hot cache, a throttled clock. **An additive shared cost is where the defence silently
inverts: it compresses toward 1, and it compresses HARDEST exactly where the arms differ most**, which
is where the number most needs to be trustworthy. **So the defence fails worst in the regime it is
relied on for.** The arithmetic: removing vsync sped CPython **4.8x** (86.5 ms -> 18 ms) and pxx
**1.1x** (436 ms -> 391 ms) — a fixed ~68 ms compositor wait is **79% of an 18 ms frame and 11% of a
391 ms one**, and that asymmetry is invisible in the ratio itself. *"I had been treating the two as the
same thing"* is true of practically everyone. **Its to write up; invited, same standard as before —
it has the artefact and the measurement.**

| 7a's, today | `object is not callable` | **a FIELD shadows a method**, call lands on the field | open — blockers 03/04 |

**Calling a field produces `object is not callable`; an arity mismatch does not.** Same exception TYPE,
different messages, different causes. **This tree already says to suspect the DIAGNOSTIC before
believing a shared cause when two subjects fail alike — and an EXCEPTION CLASS is coarser than a line
number, so the trap is BIGGER: a shared line number at least implicates one file; a shared `TypeError`
implicates nothing.** Corrected with both seats. **7a's half is untouched and settled; the note's other
half is UNMEASURED — not fixed, not refuted — and should be dated as such.**

**AND 8e's OWN CORRECTION IS THE SHARPEST SHAPE OF THE DAY AND I HAVE NOT SEEN IT STATED ANYWHERE:**

> **"I used a document's claim about itself as evidence, in the clause flagging that document as
> unreliable."**

It had written that the arity failure was *"also fixed after the old pin"* — **taken from the note's own
header and restated as established, in the same sentence quoting that note's *"Re-measure before
believing either half of this."*** **And why this is worse than an ordinary unchecked claim: a
SELF-DESCRIPTION is the first thing to go stale in a stale document** — written once, at the moment the
author is most confident, **and nothing later contradicts it because nothing later reads it.** So **the
part of a suspect document most likely to be wrong is exactly the part that reads as provenance.**
Narrow rule banked, its size right: **when you flag a document as needing re-measurement, every claim
you take FROM it inherits that flag — including the ones you are only passing along.** Mark it as the
document's claim, or measure it. **Done in the one paragraph where it was careful about everything
else — *authorship is not protection* at the scope of a single sentence, the fifth instance today and
the smallest.**

**TIER-READING DISCIPLINE WORTH THE LINE:** 8e reads `*** ... Error` and diff hunks rather than
`grep -c 'FAIL|Error'`, **which counted ten hits that were all Makefile echo text and comment prose —
the strings that WOULD print on failure.** **A log contains the vocabulary of its own failures whether
or not it failed; a grep for the word cannot tell the alarm from the wiring.** Same animal as a guard
whose baseline matches its own pattern.

**7a's ratio section strengthened at its own request (`512c9e993`):** the voided run's ratio was
**1.22x against the 1.19x already published for that scene — within 2.3%.** Two arms throttled to ~1 Hz

being wrong; it can also land on the RIGHT ANSWER FOR NO REASON, which is worse, because then it
corroborates.* **Supplied as evidence against itself.**

## ~16:40 — 7a REFUSED MY RELAY AND WAS RIGHT. RECORD THE EPISTEMIC STATE, NOT THE CURRENT BEST GUESS.

own pane.** Its sentence: *"believing a relay and asserting it in his own source are different acts,
and the last confident version of this line was also relayed and was also wrong."* **A seat that has
been wrong twice through the same channel is entitled to stop asserting through it.**

**THE DECIDING TEST, AND IT WORKS WITHOUT KNOWING WHO IS RIGHT — which is the property a rule needs
when the thing in dispute is whether to trust the source:**

> **Asserting his ruling costs something if wrong; marking it RELAYED costs nothing if right.**

`8b1a9cd` marks it **RELAYED** with the whole three-step sequence visible, **including that the
retraction was THE RELAY DOUBTING ITSELF rather than him changing his mind**, and tells a reader who
needs provenance to **ask him rather than cite the comment.** It also records that **the mechanism does
not depend on the name — a wrong spelling costs a rename and nothing else** — which is why this can sit
unresolved without blocking anything. **And it is putting the one line in front of him in its own
pane**, where he can see who is asking. That closes it firsthand for a word.

**I STATED MY OWN DIVERGENCE TO IT RATHER THAN HOLDING A LINE I DO NOT KEEP.** I wrote the name into
the pxx ticket as his ruling — **with the relay named in the same breath, the three quotes attached,
the body line struck, and *"anyone re-opening this should read his own pane, not this field"*.** My
grounds, offered for challenge: **that ticket ROUTES WORK rather than records his decision**; being
wrong costs a rename in an unimplemented module; and **I wrote the doubt into it this morning, so
leaving it OPEN would be my own stale retraction sitting at p80 where a seat picks work.** **Invited it
to tell me if that is the same error one notch quieter, in which case I mark mine RELAYED to match.**

**AND ITS CORRECTION ON WHOSE COST THIS WAS CONTAINS THE BETTER RULE:**

> **"I replaced one unsourced assertion with the opposite unsourced assertion, and that is what made the
> round trip possible. The doubt outliving its refutation needed a place to live, and my file gave it
> one."**

**RECORD THE EPISTEMIC STATE, NOT THE CURRENT BEST GUESS.** *"Unverified in either direction"* is a
fact about the EVIDENCE and stays true when the guess flips; **an assertion is a fact about the WORLD
and has to be reversed.** Had the line read that way, my later correction would have been **an update
to a field rather than a reversal** — **and a field that already says *unverified* cannot host a round
trip, because there is nothing to flip.** That is the mechanism the doubt needed in order to outlive
its refutation, and 7a found it in its own file.

**On the negative-observation rule, credit settled its way:** it had the rule from two incidents; I
only named why nobody writes it down — **a positive result proves its own reachability, so the rule is
AUTOMATIC in the case people usually meet and never earns a sentence, and it is needed exactly where an
absence is hardest to notice missing.**

tree has one dated instance — a `math.atan2` ticket found **closed by events 26 days earlier**, its own
summary recording that its blocker was gone. **Ours were hours old and caught only because two seats

**8e LANDED 7a's REACHABILITY RULE IN THE PLAYBOOK UNDER 7a's NAME**, citing `40e101c`, for the same

had the rule and **we half did**: `Assert the PRECONDITION, not just the comparison` (09-01) is a
different animal. Its table is why it earned a section rather than a clause — **three failure modes,
three different catching questions:**

| | what went wrong | the question that catches it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| inputs never produced | the comparison ran on nothing | *were the things I am comparing actually built?* |
| precondition destroyed | a setup line removed the condition under test | *does anything before the assertion stop the subject being the thing I am testing?* |
| **site never reached** | the program died earlier; the log is honestly silent | ***did execution get to the code that could produce this symptom?*** |

**Why nobody develops the habit, its words: a POSITIVE result proves its own reachability, so the skill
gets NO PRACTICE in the case where it is not needed and NO FEEDBACK in the case where it is.**

**7a IS LEAVING THE FACADES REPRODUCIBILITY QUESTION UNMEASURED AND THAT IS THE RIGHT CALL** — another
agent resident on the GPU at 1.7 GB, a nice-to-have question, **and this tree has a measured incident
of exactly that contention: two demos on one GPU, each at half frame rate, producing a plausible
table.** It would not have measured reproducibility, it would have measured the contention.
**Complete provenance for all eight images, weights on disk, reproducibility untested AND LABELLED
untested.** The owner has what the decision needs.

**THE MECHANISM RENAMES THE BLOCKER.** Not *"a field shadows a method"*: **`PyClassHeaderSweep`'s
window is ONE module's token span while the class table is global and only grows, so a call resolves
against the classes compiled SO FAR.** `traffic.py` compiles first, so when `env.wind(...)` is lowered
the only registered carrier of `wind` is `Craft`, where it is a **tuple FIELD**; `Environment`, where
it is a method, **does not exist yet.** The old code emitted a hard class cast **with no runtime class
test**, read a tuple slot off an `Environment` and called it.

**8e's CONTROL IS BETTER THAN THE WORKAROUND AND CHANGES NO CODE: IT SWAPS TWO IMPORT LINES** in
`__main__.py` and the identical program is correct. **The README's workaround ADDS an import and
therefore cannot separate *the class is registered* from *the module is imported*.** The swap can.

**AND THE SPELLING-MISSED HISTORY IS THE part worth keeping:** the **READ** path deleted these exact
two arms on 2026-09-13 — the `nc = 1` short-circuit with no test, and the last candidate as an
unconditional else — under that ticket's own rule *"the READ and the WRITE must agree about which
names dispatch."* **Read and write were made to agree. CALL is the THIRD spelling and kept both holes
for a week.** This file's *"the sibling is usually a SPELLING, not a shape"*, arriving again, in a
ticket whose own rule was one spelling short.

**AND ONE LEVEL OUT: the same import-order mechanism's REFUSAL variant was fixed 2026-09-11** by
deferring to the run-time dispatcher; this is that remedy applied to the **SILENT-MISBIND** variant.
8e's own generalisation, and it is the sharpest sentence in the message: **a refusal announces itself,
a misbind does not — so the refusal was found nine days earlier.**

**THE INVERTED CORRELATION IS A LIVE TRAP AND I ASKED FOR IT AT THE SOURCE, NOT IN A MESSAGE.**
Measured at `3c6e31f94a62d295`: **the repro emits ZERO warnings and fails; the `control_field_renamed`
control emits SIX and is correct.** `no class declares a method or callable field .X()` **fires when
the scan finds NOTHING and defers to run time — the SAFE outcome.** The defect is the scan finding
something **wrong** and binding silently. **Anyone grepping that warning is chasing the arm that
already works, and the blockers README currently points them there.**

**MY 06 HYPOTHESIS SURVIVED AND WAS INVERTED, WHICH MADE IT USEFUL.** I offered 03/06 as one family
and flagged it as a hypothesis. 8e agrees on the family and **reverses the direction: 03 is a decision
taken with too FEW classes registered; 06 is a wrong bind appearing when one MORE is.** That is what
kills the repair anyone would reach for — *make the class table complete before lowering anything* —
because **06 stands as evidence that a fuller table is not automatically safer and the repair could
have promoted a latent bug to an always-on one.** 8e has NOT measured 06 and does not claim it.

**8e's OWN 05 HYPOTHESIS WAS REFUTED BY 8e's OWN PROBE AND IT RECORDED THAT RATHER THAN DROPPING
IT.** It predicted the read-path twin (`pydynattr_get` walks store, fields, properties, not methods),
built the shape, and **it PASSES, matching CPython.** Which is what 05's README already predicts — it
needs the demo's population. **That is the third reduction that fails to capture 05**, and 7a's *"05
survived the 03 workaround"* stands unchallenged. **Nobody has a reproduction of 05 smaller than the
demo.** Recording a refutation matters here specifically because **a passing reduction is the shape
that reads as "fixed".**

**INSTRUMENT FINDING, AND IT IS AN EXTENSION OF A RULE THIS FILE ALREADY HAS.** 8e's fixed compiler
**FAILED** the demo build at `heapq.heapify` while the unfixed one built clean — **a perfect
regression signature, produced entirely by the binary's LOCATION.** pxx resolves the RTL relative to
its own directory (`./compiler/../lib/rtl/`), so a compiler in a scratchpad **cannot see
`mimic_heapq` at all**; identical bytes copied into `compiler/` built fine. **CLAUDE.md covers the
BUILTIN lookup for a relocated binary, including the silent-substitution arm. It does not cover the
LIBRARY lookup, which is a SECOND search with a different scope and NO CWD-relative rescue** — so
*"run it from the repo root"* is obeyed, sufficient for the rule as written, **and still wrong: the
binary has to SIT in the checkout.** 8e nearly reported its own fix as a regression; **someone else
would have reported it and been believed.**

kind. Second-independent-subsystem fails — one seat, one binary, one day. Playbook, attributed.
HELD until 8e says whether it wants to write it itself; I offered, and landing it while it drafts is
exactly the collision I exist to prevent.** What would promote it: **a differently-scoped resolution
path hit from a relocated binary in another lane.**

`done/`. `PXX_CLONE_THREAD` omitted `CLONE_SETTLS`, so a pxx-created thread inherited the parent's
`fs` base — **glibc's thread pointer, holding per-thread malloc state it takes no lock on** — and two
threads allocating through C corrupted the heap. **A compiled-NilPy binary crashing under SDL,
driver-sensitive, CPython twin surviving: the note's shape.**

**(b) IT IS A RACE, SO N=1 IS INSIDE THE NOISE.** *"Five aborts gave THREE different glibc messages,
which is the race."* 8e's negative is **non-vacuous and I agree with its reasoning** — execution
demonstrably **reached the site** (GL up, world loaded, legend printed: exactly the state the note
says it dies *after*) and ran 45 s past it. **What "reached the site" licenses is the second clause,
not the strength.** Against a 3/5-to-5/5 intermittent, **one clean run proves little; five would.**
Otherwise the honest sentence is *"did not reproduce in 1 run"*. **8e explicitly did NOT claim its fix
cured it — different symptoms, one run — and did not file a bug for something that did not
reproduce.**

## ~19:40 — I RAN 8e's "I WOULD NOT ASSUME THIS IS THE ONLY ONE". IT IS THE ONLY ONE: 1 OF 23.

**8e took the residual it did not own, measured it, and was firm that the count is not a verdict —
which is the right instinct and is the opposite of how a clean number usually gets reported.** Eight
runs, zero failures, **recorded in `runbin.sh`'s own comment block in that file's dated-measurement
style (`feb6518`) plus two LOGBOOK lines here — at the artefact, not in a message.**

    wayland  5 x 25s   GL 3.3 up, full legend, 5/5 alive
    x11      3 x 30s   GL 3.3 up, full legend, 3/3 alive

All eight killed by their own timeout, no fault, no glibc corruption string. **THREE claims in one
comment block not reproducing, not one:** the wayland segfault, a 2026-09-13 x11 HANG
(`SDL_CreateWindow NEVER RETURNS`), and **the logbook's 2026-09-14 x11 3/5 abort.** It added the x11
arm **because of the polarity conflict I flagged**, and the conflict **dissolves rather than
resolving**: neither driver dies for this binary.

**ITS CORRECTION TO MY N=1 CAUTION IS SHARPER THAN MY CAUTION AND I AM RECORDING IT AS ITS OWN:** the
fix is not 1-versus-8. **A negative against an INTERMITTENT needs its RATE quoted; a negative against
a DETERMINISTIC claim does not.** 5/5 wayland against *"reaches GL 3.3 and THEN segfaults"* is strong;
3/3 x11 against a 3/5 abort is **~6% — suggestive, not decisive.** *"Two different evidential
situations that the single word 'clean' hides."* **And the caveat that makes the note re-usable is its
own: its binary carries a compiler fix the original did not, so *"already stale"* and *"something
cured it"* are NOT SEPARABLE by this measurement.** My `CLONE_SETTLS` lead is in the note under my
name, marked not established — **which it is not, by me either.** Standing: **measured, not retired.
8e owns the MEASUREMENT and explicitly not the RESOLUTION.**

**THE CENSUS — because its closing line was a testable claim and testing it is cheaper than assuming
either way.** *"Whenever a downstream repo names one of our slugs, whether we actually have it — I
would not assume this is the only one."*

**Oracle:** 4,945 slugs, every `.md` filename under `devdocs/progress/*/` at HEAD. **Population:** ten
non-pxx repos on this box. **Excluded `trackt-watch` and `pxx-songfmt` — pxx clones citing 1,805 and
1,587 slugs, i.e. their own `devdocs/` copies, not downstream citations.** Two regexes, strict and
loose, **because the strict lane-letter form cannot see `bug-nilpy-*` or `docs-*` slugs and I did not
want the instrument's blind spot doing the reassuring.**

**RESULT: 23 distinct citations, 3 repos citing anything at all, 1 genuinely absent — 8e's.** The two
other apparent misses were **instrument artefacts and are recorded as such rather than dropped:** my
own regex **truncating a slug at a line wrap** (that ticket is in `done/`), and **six slug-shaped
strings in `pxx-website`'s test fixtures** — `scripts/check-content-resilience.py` writing synthetic
`devdocs/progress/done/*.md` files to test its own parser. **Never citations.**

**So the class is REAL and its population is ONE. Both halves are the finding** — real means the hole
is genuine and ownerless; **one means it does not justify a mechanism, and that is what stops someone
building a cross-repo slug checker for a population of one.** **The limit I could not close:** the
census finds only what is **textually a slug**. A defect described downstream **with no slug at all**
is invisible to it and to everything else — **and that is the commoner shape, because writing a slug
is the conscientious version.**

**8e's FRAMING IS THE FINDING AND IT BEAT MINE: a defect recorded in the CONSUMER's artefact instead
of the producer's backlog.** It reads as tracked **because it has a ticket number**, and `ready`,
`next`, the ranker and `check` **all read our own tree**, so it **has no owner by construction.** It
surfaced only because a seat ran the consumer's script in-form — **the same rule earning its keep
twice in one afternoon.**

**04 IS TAKEN — p70, the thing between seeing it and sailing it — AND THE FIXTURE AXIS IS THE BEST
FINDING OF THE DAY.** `drive(Boat())` **PASSES**; `b = Boat(); drive(b)` **FAILS**. A fresh
construction lets the frontend type the parameter from the call site, so **the receiver stops being
dynamic** — and the defect only exists for a dynamic receiver. **8e wrote the passing spelling FIRST,
for the best possible reason: constructing in the call is the natural way to make a test
self-contained.** Five rows, controls, **reproducing nothing** — and **written after a fix instead of
before one it would have certified 04 as repaired.**

**Same family as CLAUDE.md's *"the position of the interesting element is a variable"*, new axis: not
WHERE the interesting element sits but WHERE THE VALUE CAME FROM.** A literal, a fresh construction,
an inline expression **each hand the compiler more static information than a bound variable does, and
each is the tidier way to write the fixture — so the tidiness and the blindness arrive together**,
which is why reviewing a fixture for quality does not catch it. **Promotion: merit yes, extension
rather than neighbour, second-subsystem NO. Playbook, flagged — one more subsystem where a fixture's
natural spelling supplies static information the real call site does not, and it goes up.**

**AND frankH's CLAMPING ROW IS THE WHOLE FIXTURE.** A setter clamping to 1.0, handed 4.0, **reads back
`1.0` if it ran and `4.0` if it never did**; pxx gives `(4.0, 0.0)` — the 4.0 straight back out of the
shadow, real field untouched. **Every other row can be satisfied by a shadow answering its own write**,
which is exactly what makes the defect silent, **so a fixture without that row goes green while the
setter is never called.** The other rows are not weak — they are **structurally unable to observe the
thing.**

**AND A TICKET ASSERTED ITS OWN SAFEKEEPING FALSELY:** the 04 parking note says *"its source is inline
in this ticket"* and **there is no code block in the file.** Load-bearing — the same paragraph says the
fixture lived in a scratchpad that does not survive a reboot, **so that sentence IS the safeguard and
it was not taken.** 8e recovered it from the demo repo's four probes and **recorded the discrepancy in
the ticket rather than quietly rebuilding**, which is right: **a ticket asserting a thing was preserved
gets planned around.**

**LANDED: `e03041e5e`** — both playbook sections, quoted and attributed to 8e, census included with
its oracle and population. **Relay still held. Tier 395/~1045, 0 errors, 0 diff hunks.**

> **A diagnostic written to STDOUT is capturable, and anything that captures stdout to read ONE value

> path was built to extract. One capture, two paths, and only one of them was designed.**

reader who checks it finds it correct and moves on. **5b's phrase: "right idea, one step short."**

## ~21:00 — 5b TOOK THE PEN AND WAS RIGHT TO; IT CORRECTED MY CAVEAT FRAMING; AND MY CENSUS OF ITS

**I offered to write 5b's finding into the playbook and it wrote it itself — `e3c4ae9cc`, verified as

removed.** It had the tree open; it cost three minutes.

**ITS CORRECTION TO MY CAVEAT FRAMING IS ACCEPTED AND IS SHARPER THAN MINE.** I recorded the
narrowing of *"the LED is unwitnessed"* to *"the LED is unwitnessed, the PROGRAM is not"* as
**restraint** — a caveat nobly shrunk. **5b says it was not restraint: it was a caveat OVERSIZED in
the first place**, written while it had looked only at the LED, **re-measured only because my question
sent it back to the file** instead of to its own earlier sentence.

> **A caveat written while you were looking at one half of a thing inherits that half's scope, and
> nothing in it announces which half.**

**That explains what my version could not: why nobody re-measures a caveat.** It does not read as
partial. *"The LED is unwitnessed"* is a complete-sounding statement, and the missing words — *"and I
have not looked at the rest"* — are **exactly the words an author has no reason to write.** My framing
made it a virtue; **5b's makes it a mechanism, and a mechanism has a remedy.** Recorded as 5b's.

**AND ITS FIRST ADDITION RETIRES MY OWN SUMMARY OF ITS BUG. I wrote it up as a `set -e` ordering
problem; `set -e` is incidental.**

> **Whenever you capture or parse a program's output to extract a value, you have taken over
> responsibility for its error channel whether you meant to or not.** The value you wanted and the
> diagnostic you did not think about **come down the same pipe.**

**The bug existing BECAUSE of a feature is what makes it survive review** — the capture was added for
a legitimate need, correctly implemented, and **took the failure path's only output channel with it**
as a side effect nobody wrote down. **`capture_output`, a pipeline into `grep`, a CI step scraping a
number out of a log: all the same animal, and none of them LOOK like error handling**, which is why
none get reviewed as error handling.

**I CENSUSED THE CLASS THE WAY I DID 8e's, AND THE HONEST RESULT IS THAT THE CENSUS CANNOT ANSWER
IT — recorded as a shape, deliberately not as a count.** The idiom is everywhere: `uses_sweep.sh`,
`ok_line_means_a_file.sh`, `selfhost_stamp_devtest.sh`, `elf_reader_vs_readelf.sh`,
`fpc_compiler_corpus_probe.sh` all capture a compiler or `make` invocation into a variable. **None of
them sets `-e`; two never print the captured variable at all.** But **"captures output" plus "never
prints it" is the defect's SHAPE, not the defect** — separating them needs someone to read or run each
failure branch. **A number here would have been the census-whose-filter-restates-the-hypothesis
failure I would flag in anyone else**, so: **the idiom is common** — and **BOTH of the other two
clauses I wrote here were WRONG, corrected below at ~21:35 by 5b, which went and read the five failure
branches instead of leaving the shape open.** `set -e` **is** part of the discriminator, and the tree
**does** say which instances are live; I was reading the wrong artefact.

**WHICH LANDS ON 5b's SECOND ADDITION, AND IT IS WHY ITS ENTRY HAS AN ACTION WHERE MINE WOULD HAVE HAD
A CAUTION:**

> **A failure path is code nobody has ever executed.** A success path runs every day; a failure path
> runs **the first time it is needed, by whoever needed it, under time pressure** — here, the owner
> with a board in his hand. **Run the failure path once, on purpose.** It costs a minute and it is the
> only thing that finds this class.

**The instrument failed and the remedy did not, and that is the finding: a census cannot find these; a
minute of deliberate breakage can.**

**Two entries today where I offered to write a peer's finding and the peer wrote it better from the
tree** (8e cleared me to land its RTL-location section and I did; 5b took the pen and should have).
**The rule I am taking from the pair: offer, and prefer the seat with the tree open.** A coordinator
landing a finding is a hop, and **a hop is only worth adding when the author cannot land it
themselves.**

**634 ok rows, ZERO diff hunks, ZERO expect_same mismatches, and exactly one failure: a build error

**I CHECKED WHETHER ANYTHING ALREADY IN FRONT OF HIM HAD GONE STALE. IT HAD NOT — recorded explicitly

**THE INSTRUMENT FINDING IS WORTH MORE THAN 04 AND IS THE RESULT OF THE RUN.** 8e **held off rebuilding
the binary specifically to obey *do not touch the instrument while it is measuring*** — the rule being
applied, not ignored — **and it was not enough:**

> **`compiler/builtin/pylib.pas` is not compiled INTO the compiler; it is READ FROM THE TREE by every
> single test compile.** So **the binary was correctly frozen and the instrument moved anyway, through
> an input nobody had classified as one.**

**The rule 8e had in mind was "do not REBUILD"; the rule as written is "do not TOUCH", and the gap
between those two is where this landed.** That is an **extension** of a CLAUDE.md rule rather than a
new one: **the instrument is not the binary, it is every input the run reads — and a tree-read input
is invisible precisely because freezing the binary FEELS like freezing the instrument.** It
generalises to any harness whose subject loads data, templates or plugins from the working tree.
**Promotion: merit yes, extension not neighbour, second-subsystem no. Playbook when 8e is free to
write it, or by me with its clearance — and after today's two cases I offer and prefer the seat with
the tree open.**

**AND 8e's CAVEAT ON ITS OWN NEAR MISS IS WHAT MAKES IT USEFUL: IT DID NOT CHOOSE THE ORDER THAT
FAILED LOUDLY.** Forward declaration first gave `unresolved forward` **at build time**; **implementation
first would have COMPILED and the tier would have measured a half-written routine** — a wrong value 600
rows deep instead of a build error. *"The order that crashes is the safe one here, which is not a
general property and is not something I chose."* **A near miss reported as a near miss rather than as
a safeguard.**

rows 1-2.
