## ~21:35 — 5b CLOSED THE CENSUS I LEFT OPEN: ZERO OF FIVE ARE INSTANCES. AND BOTH OF MY OTHER TWO
## CLAUSES WERE WRONG.

**`43f2ad304` verified as an ancestor before logging.** I recorded a SHAPE and declined a count, which
was right. **5b went and read the five failure branches — about ten minutes — and answered it
completely.** **Zero of the five are instances, and the four different reasons are the useful part:**

    ok_line_means_a_file.sh        tests $?, PRINTS the captured text on the failure branch, and
                                   guards the empty-capture case explicitly
    fpc_compiler_corpus_probe.sh   redirects to a FILE, so the diagnostic survives
    selfhost_stamp_devtest.sh      asserts on the CONTENT, positive control per arm
    elf_reader_vs_readelf.sh       records $? at every capture
    uses_sweep.sh                  captures no compiler invocation at all

**`ok_line_means_a_file.sh` is not merely safe, it is the WORKED EXAMPLE** — it does every single
thing the new entry prescribes, **including the empty-capture guard** (*"could not read
`code=`/`codeseg=` off the `ok:` line — nothing was compared"*), **and it was written months before
any of this.** 5b put the table in the entry because **"here are five scripts that get it right, and
how" teaches better than the one that got it wrong.**

**CORRECTION ONE, AND IT IS 5b CORRECTING ITSELF: `set -e` IS NOT INCIDENTAL. I RELAYED THAT CLAIM
TWO ENTRIES AGO AND IT WAS TOO STRONG.** The live defect is **a conjunction of three**: a diagnostic
on **stdout**, a **capture** that takes it, **and `set -e`**. **None of the five sets `-e`, and that is
exactly why a failed capture there surfaces loudly** — the script continues, the content assertion
fails, somebody sees it. **What `set -e` decides is not whether the bug exists but WHICH failure you
get: with it, silence; without it, the script proceeds holding an empty variable and reports a wrong
answer.** The second is **not better, only louder** — **and what turns a wrong answer into a visible
one is a positive control, which all five have and `build.sh` did not.** The ownership half
generalises past shell exactly as written and is untouched.

**CORRECTION TWO, AND IT IS 5b CORRECTING ME: MY "NOTHING IN THE TREE SAYS WHICH INSTANCES ARE LIVE"
WAS WRONG, AND ITS REASON IS SHARPER THAN MINE.**

> **The grep returns five clean scripts and one live bug with NO WAY TO TELL THEM APART, because the
> IDIOM is the shape and the FAILURE BRANCH is the defect. The instrument was not weak; it was AIMED
> AT THE WRONG ARTEFACT.**

**I concluded the question was unanswerable when it was answerable by reading a different thing.**
That is a distinct instrument failure from the ones in this file — **not stale, not the wrong
population, not a filter restating the hypothesis: correctly executed against an artefact that does
not contain the answer** — and it is the one that produces a confident *"cannot be determined"*.
**Both of my clauses are corrected in place above, with the error left visible.**

**AND THE REASON IT MATTERED, WHICH IS THE PART I WOULD HAVE MISSED: AN OVERSTATED OPEN CLASS IS A
TAX.** 5b: *"a class recorded as open and common quietly taxes every future reader of those five
scripts."* **The honest score is one instance, found, fixed, and the idiom in good shape elsewhere** —
**smaller and more reassuring than the shape implied.** I had written the cautious-sounding version,
and **cautious-sounding is not free**: every future reader of those five scripts would have paid for
my refusal to spend ten minutes.

**Two corrections accepted from 5b in one evening, both against sentences of mine that READ as
careful.** The pattern in both: **a hedge I was pleased with, standing in for a measurement that was
cheap.**

## ~21:55 — 5b RE-SCOPED A TRUE ANSWER NOBODY HAD QUESTIONED. AND OUR TWO INSTRUMENT FAILURES ARE ONE
## ROOT WITH OPPOSITE VERDICTS — THE ASYMMETRY IS THE FINDING.

**5b completed its ESP answer in the one direction I had not checked, unprompted.** *"Every NilPy ESP
demo prints to UART"* was accurate and I relayed it accurately — **and the NilPy four are exactly the
demos the PIN CANNOT BUILD.** So **his most likely first flash is a PASCAL demo, `hello-c3`, which sat
outside the population of the answer, and nothing in the answer said so.** That is 5b's own rule from
an hour earlier landing in its own work: **a caveat written while you were looking at one half
inherits that half's scope, and nothing in it announces which half.** Found the same way both times —
**a question sending it back to the file. This time it sent itself.**

**THE EXTENSION, RELAYED UPWARD AS THE LEAD: ALL NINE PASCAL ESP DEMOS PRINT OVER UART.** `hello-c3`
emits **five numbered lines plus a sum** via `esp_rom_printf`, then **parks in a `vTaskDelay` loop so
the watchdog stays fed**; same for `gpio-c3`, `timer-c3`, `net-c3`, `fs-c3`, `dns-c3`, `hello-s3`,
`timer-s3`, `hello-s2`. **Pass-from-hang discriminates for EVERY ESP demo in the tree, including the
ones he can build today.**

**Two differences, relayed as differences and not as blockers.** **(1)** The Pascal demos have **no
`main.expected` and no `qemu-assert`** — `build.sh` takes `qemu`, running `idf.py qemu monitor`
interactively, so they are **eyeball-comparable, not byte-comparable.** **(2)** The two shelves have
**OPPOSITE compiler defaults**: Pascal hardcodes `PXX="$REPO_ROOT/compiler/pascal26"` (HEAD, no
override); NilPy defaults to the **PIN** and takes an override. **The Pascal default is the one that
works**, so the likely first flash needs nothing special.

**AND THE SIXTH DISTINCT REASON IS THE MORAL OF THE WHOLE THREAD: the Pascal scripts DO NOT CAPTURE
THE COMPILE AT ALL.** `set -euo pipefail` plus an uncaptured invocation **aborts loudly with the
diagnostic on the terminal.** 5b: ***"that is the simplest correct answer of all, and it is what
`build.sh` looked like before somebody needed one value off the `ok:` line."*** **The bug was
introduced by a requirement; the pre-requirement code was already right.**

**THE PAIR I AM RECORDING INSTEAD OF THE TWO INSTANCES.** 5b grepped `WriteLn` to ask whether the
Pascal demos print, got **ZERO across nine**, and for a moment had a finding — *nine demos that emit
nothing, flash into silence*, **exactly the gap being hunted.** They print with `esp_rom_printf`,
declared `external`, **because there is no console before IDF's init**: CLAUDE.md's *"grep for the
OTHER SPELLING'S HANDLER, not for the feature"*, **tripped while checking somebody else's coverage.**
Mine greped the idiom instead of the failure branch and returned *cannot be determined*.

> **Both instruments ran correctly against an artefact that did not contain the answer. One produced
> a confident FALSE ALARM; the other a confident CANNOT-BE-DETERMINED. The false alarm is the one that
> gets caught, because somebody eventually checks it. The cannot-be-determined SURVIVES, because it
> sounds like rigour and nobody audits a non-answer.**

**5b's would have cost an alarm and been corrected within the hour. Mine would have cost a standing
tax on five clean scripts, indefinitely.** **That asymmetry is the finding**, and it is why the
cautious-sounding verdict is the more expensive error of the two.

**MOVED TO THE PLAYBOOK BY 5b, `77b6a2d2d`, BOTH ROWS AND BOTH NAMES — AND ITS REASON IS A CORRECTION
TO MY METHOD ALL DAY, NOT TO THIS ENTRY: "a steering log is not an artefact anyone greps."** Left
here, the sharpest thing from tonight would have been **recorded correctly and read by nobody, which
is a non-answer's cost in a different shape.** See ~22:10.**

**Labelled by 5b as scope correction, not a new finding, and relayed that way. No change landed; none
needed. The UART claim stays open, now covering nine more demos on the same qemu-and-source-read
footing, and the first flash tests it.**

## ~01:35 — MY PROMOTION ARGUMENT WAS DECLINED AND THE DECLINE WAS CORRECT. A COORDINATOR'S ARGUMENT
## FOR PROMOTION NEEDS A HIGHER BAR, NOT THE SAME ONE.

**I argued the denial-comment remedy up on the grounds that the REMEDY recurs in a second subsystem.
5b declined it and recorded the decline IN the entry (`30d8c5ec6`), with the trigger stated.**

**It is right and my argument was the thing to decline.** The FAILURE has **one instance** — one
denial comment, one grep, today. **I substituted a property of the FIX for a property of the
RECURRENCE**, which is precisely the substitution CLAUDE.md names as how that file reached 72KB the
first time: **a finding argued up on how good it is rather than on whether it came back.** And I made
it **at the end of a day in which I had held that exact line five times** against work at least as
good — which is data about when the discipline slips, not about this finding.

**THE THING WORTH KEEPING IS THE OBSERVATION 5b MADE MOST GENTLY, AND IT IS ABOUT THIS SEAT:**

> **"I notice the argument is more persuasive coming from you than it would be from me, which is its
> own reason to be careful with it."**

**Same asymmetry I spent the evening worrying about in the ENDORSEMENT direction, which I never
thought to apply to PROMOTION.** A coordinator arguing FOR a promotion is not one voice among several
— **it carries the weight of the seat that has been adjudicating everyone else's promotions all day,
and that weight is borrowed from the DECLINES rather than earned by the argument.** So:

> **The bar on a promotion argued by the coordinator must be HIGHER than the standard one, not equal
> to it.** It takes the person on the receiving end to see this; I would not have.

**And recording the decision IN the entry, with the trigger, is the half I would have skipped** — *a
second denial comment, unrelated subsystem, found to have armed a search for the construct it promises
is absent.* **So the next reader neither reconstructs the reasoning nor re-argues it from scratch.**

**5b ALSO TOOK THE NARROWER RESIDUAL RATHER THAN ABSORBING IT**, and the reason that matters: had it
let *"endorse less"* stand, **I would have taken the lesson that makes this seat less useful instead
of the one that makes it more.** *"What has been RUN, not only what has been measured"* is in the
standing set.

**CLEAN STOP, LEDGER AS HELD:** tree clean; **gate GREEN from the job's own line**; both chains on one
binary; nothing inert pending a pin; **lekkerzeilen's two blockers on origin with one unmet row that
is the owner's**; **ESP ready for silicon** with the board as the single open claim, everything around
it exercised.

## 5b handed me a `decide`; I measured it and gave it back as engineering

**`5b10e823c` is live for every lane: `gate.sh`'s test-wiring row is disarmed by the push.**
`tools/check_test_wiring.py --since "origin/$GATE_BRANCH"` (`gate.sh:941-944`) enumerates tests added
since origin, so once you push, it reports *"0 test file(s) added"* and **prints PASS having examined
nothing.** Both 5b's gate runs said PASS for a test neither could see. **Reproduced on my own clean
tree: rc=0, zero files.**

**5b's framing is the part worth keeping and it is not about the checker:** *this project's own
correct advice produces the disarming order.* **Push often. A local commit is not banking. Push
before a measurement starts, never during one.** All three right, all three landing on push-then-gate.
**The window that works is narrow — commit → gate → push, because committed-but-unpushed IS visible
to the guard.**

**IT DID NOT CHANGE THE GUARD, AND OFFERED ME THE `decide` IN GOAL TERMS** — *"should a green gate be
able to mean 'your new test is wired' even when you banked your work first?"* **Its stated reason for
holding was that the change is the owner's direction of travel to approve.**

**THAT READING IS BACKWARDS AND I CORRECTED IT, RUNNING IN 5b's FAVOUR:** CLAUDE.md is *"Tightening is
yours. LOOSENING is the owner's."* Making a guard look at a WIDER range is **tightening** — a seat's
to do. **Its second reason was the sound one and sufficient alone:** an unmeasured change to a guard
every lane runs is a **coordination** question, not a permission one. Correcting it matters because
the wrong reading would stop that seat tightening something else later.

**SO THE COORDINATOR'S MOVE WAS THE POPULATION QUESTION IT COULD NOT ANSWER FROM INSIDE ITS OWN
CHECKOUT** — *how many lanes would suddenly start seeing rows they have been passing for months?*
The checker has a **census mode** (no `--since`). **Measured: census rc=1, exactly TWO unwired files**,
both pre-existing, neither anyone's work today; 43 parked against a live ticket; 2 named-only
exemptions. **Numbers and the ordering consequence are in `debugging-playbook.md` (`f9925d468`),
appended to 5b's own section — this entry does not restate them.**

**WHAT THE MEASUREMENT DID IS CHANGE THE KIND OF QUESTION IT IS.** CLAUDE.md: *cost is not the fork;
two options priced against each other is engineering.* **An unmeasured blast radius reads as a fork of
intent; a measured one is a sequence** — wire or exempt two files, then the stricter mode is adoptable
at zero cost to every lane. **Nothing goes upward. No `decide` filed.** That is the whole discharge of
*"the productive move is not to implement and not merely to escalate — MEASURE, so his call is narrow
instead of an architecture fork"*, in the version where the measurement removes the call entirely.

**AND I DID NOT TOUCH THE TWO FILES.** I do not know why they are unwired, and **guessing a reason
into `test/UNWIRED.txt` would be worse than the gap** — the exemption file's whole value is that its
reasons are true. Measured, named, handed back.

**MY OWN FIRST ATTEMPT AT THAT MEASUREMENT WAS CORRUPTED BY TODAY'S OWN TRAP.** `| tail -25; echo
"rc=$?"` gave **tail's 0** over the checker's real **1**. **Third instance on this box today, in the
seat that relayed the other two within the hour.** I caught it only because the number was friendlier
than the output looked — which is the tell worth carrying: **a status that agrees with what you were
hoping for is the one to re-measure.** Reported to 5b in the same message as the finding rather than
kept, because a seat that reports only its peers' instrument failures is teaching the wrong lesson.

## Chasing one residual surfaced five invisible defects — and the mechanism had been called 23 days earlier

**5b put a residual in my lane: *if a limited+full red reaches nobody, why?*** Measured, and the
answer corrected its framing and then mine. **The tier was not unread. It ran, went red, and reported
faithfully — 183 times — and was structurally unable to say anything had CHANGED.**

`tools-devtest` is an aggregate publishing under the opaque shard `tools-devtest#00`. Continuously
red since **2026-09-12**, **182 consecutive `still_red`, zero transitions**. **A saturated aggregate
cannot report a new red inside it.** Numbers, oracle and the named guards are in
`debugging-playbook.md` ("AN AGGREGATE JOB SATURATES"); this entry does not restate them.

**Five real defects were sitting behind that one unchanging level**, landed across at least three
days: an expectation drift across three targets; **a binary-name collision where one test's assertion
runs another test's program**; two tests writing hardcoded `/tmp` paths; and 5b's unwired-test
omission. **All named, all dated, all now owned** — `8c32b5b7d` repairs a stale ticket summary,
`b7d1e125b` files the two that had none.

**THE PART THAT MATTERS FOR HOW THIS SEAT WORKS, AND IT IS NOT THE FINDING.** I wrote the mechanism up
as a discovery. **It was predicted on 2026-08-28**, in the *Boundaries* section of the very ticket I
was repairing, as its stated reason for its prio: *"a red gate that everyone knows about stops being
read."* **One `grep -rl` found it, after I had written two paragraphs.** So the rule I want carried
forward is: **when you find a hazard, go and find who already called it, and attach your evidence to
THEIR ticket instead of opening a new page.** What I actually contributed was the **price** — a
predicted failure with no number stays at p45; the same sentence with 182 rows and five victims is a
different ticket. That is worth having, and it is not what I claimed.

**FOUR QUANTIFIERS IN ONE EVENING WERE ESTIMATED RATHER THAN COUNTED, ALL MINE.** *"the only one being
asked"* (false), *"exactly one file fails"* (two), *"~80 guards"* (**166**), and the discovery framing
above. **The second and third were typed one paragraph after I recorded what the habit costs**, in a
section correcting me for it. **Knowing the rule, having just written it, and being the person
correcting someone else for breaking it are all compatible with breaking it in the next paragraph.**
The hedge is the only thing that saved them — I said *"a later FAIL would change the word exactly"*
before the run finished, and it did. **Hedging is not a substitute for counting; it is what makes the
number recoverable when you were wrong.**

**AND THE WRAPPER LIED ONCE MORE, AFTER ALL OF IT** — the background notification read `completed
(exit code 0)`; `make` exited **2**; the job's own line said `163 green, 2 RED`. **Fourth instance in
one day.** The only reason it cost nothing is that I had already decided to grep for the job's verdict.

**5b DECLINED THE PROMOTION I DID NOT ARGUE FOR, AND ITS REASON IS BETTER THAN MY RESTRAINT WAS.** I
held back because a coordinator's promotion argument needs a higher bar. **Its reason is structural:
a file every session is charged for at startup should trace to the OWNER, not to two agents agreeing
with each other at the end of a long day** — and today is exactly the day we would both be most
confident and least placed to notice. It banked the evidence so his call costs one sentence. **That is
the better formulation and it is now the one I hold.**

**Its correction of my asymmetry is adopted too:** *you corrected me on things I could have checked
and did not; I corrected you on things you could not check from where you sit.* **One tree and one
lane against no tree and every lane.** Neither error class is the better contribution, and reading
them as a scoreboard is the third time today I have been pulled off scorekeeping.

**LEDGER: unchanged upward.** No `decide` filed, nothing escalated. Tree clean, everything pushed,
gate untouched. **lekkerzeilen's one unmet row is still the owner's**, ESP still waits on silicon, his
three questions still relayed and waiting. **De-saturating `tools-devtest` is what stops the sixth
defect hiding, and it belongs to a lane, not to this seat.**

## Closing 09-20: four wrong explanations, four corrections, none settled by argument

**The entry above ends on the version of the evening where I was right. I was not, and the record
should not close there.**

**CORRECTED 2026-09-20, AFTER THE FACT, AND THE CORRECTION REVERSES ITSELF ONCE — read to the end
before believing any middle paragraph.** I proposed a mechanical cause for a counting disagreement:
`tools-devtest`'s `tail -25` had dropped a row. 5b replayed it and produced a table refuting it; **I
accepted the refutation inside one message, withdrew a true finding, banked a fresh self-criticism
explaining an error I had not made, and corrected a `done/` ticket into a false state.** 5b then
replayed it a second time the way the recipe actually runs it — **stdout redirected to a FILE** — and
**the original finding is CORRECT**: at three findings the log is 28 lines, `tail -25` drops the
blank, the FAIL header and the first finding row, and the survivors are exactly the two `.npy` files
reported. `test/lib_findfirst.pas` is the row that went.

**Its refutation failed on ONE TRAILING NEWLINE:** it captured the guard with `out=$(python3 ...)`,
and **command substitution strips trailing newlines**, so it measured a 27-line stream where the file
is 28. Every number in its table was off by one in the same direction, which is why it looked
internally consistent.

**AND MY OWN `24` WAS A BOUNDARY ERROR IN THE SAME THREE LINES, WHICH I CLOSED FROM MY OWN SAVED
LOG.** The `tail -25` block is lines **85–109 = 25**, a full window; I counted **85–108**, excluding
the trailing blank the guard prints. **My original "exactly 25" used 84–108 — including the wrapper's
echo, excluding the trailing blank — two errors that cancelled to the right answer.** The same
ambiguity resolved three ways, each time landing on whatever hypothesis was in the room. Banked at
`7fb6a46c3` and re-aimed afterwards; 5b's retraction is `f3dec7d9b`.

**THE CAUSE OF TWO-VERSUS-THREE IS STILL UNKNOWN AND THE SLOT IS DELIBERATELY EMPTY.** Two
explanations were offered and both withdrawn. **Neither of us filled it with a third**, and that is
the part a future coordinator should copy: a `done/` ticket is the worst resting place an unmeasured
claim can reach, because nothing re-reads it and nothing re-runs it.

**WHAT ACTUALLY WORKED, FOUR TIMES, AND IT WAS NEVER ARGUMENT.** A `grep -rl` for prior art; a line
count against a saved log; a replay of an old script against restored sources; a positive control on a
scratch file. **Not one correction this evening came from reasoning harder — every one came from going
back to an artefact.** So when a correction and a counter-correction are both plausible, **do not weigh
them: ask what artefact still exists that would decide it.** In this repo there almost always is one.

**THE SHARPEST THING PRODUCED TODAY IS 5b's AND IT BELONGS IN THIS LOG UNDER ITS NAME:** *an
explanation more satisfying than the gap it fills is the one to replay — satisfaction reaches the
receiver long before verification does, and a shrug is the shape a true answer often has.* My
truncation story had a number, a derivation, a named mechanism and a moral. **The honest answer was a
shrug.** My own version of the receiving-end rule — *a verified headline lends credibility to an
unverified cause* — is true and softer, and I would keep 5b's over mine.

**NO SCOREKEEPING, AND THIS IS THE FIFTH TIME TODAY I HAVE BEEN PULLED OFF IT** — by 5b, who declined
to restart it first. **What is worth recording is not who was wrong how often: it is that neither seat
spent a turn defending.** That is why four wrong explanations cost one evening rather than sitting in
`done/` permanently, and it is not a property of either seat alone.

**LEDGER AT CLOSE, unchanged upward all evening:** nothing escalated, no `decide` filed, gate
untouched, no pin, **no guard loosened**. `165 guard(s) green, zero red`; gate quick GREEN; tree clean.
**Five defects invisible this morning are fixed and `tools-devtest` can report an event again** — that
result is 5b's. **Still the owner's, still waiting:** lekkerzeilen's one unmet row, `assets/facades.lzx`,
the four untracked PNGs, whether to pin. **ESP waits on silicon, and the UART filter is the one claim
no instrument here can close.**

## 09-20 night: a relay chain where three explanations were wrong, and a hazard the census nearly cleared

**THE CENSUS THAT WAS RIGHT ABOUT THE WRONG CLASS IS THE ENTRY WORTH READING.** Everything
else here is the same shape a fourth and fifth time.

**Backticks inside a double-quoted `git commit -m "..."` are command substitution.** Bash runs
them, with the seat's full permissions, before git sees the string. 8e hit the loud version
(`11a846d4d`, 40 lines of `git show` spliced into a message), disclosed it unprompted, and
priced the damage as cosmetic — *"not worth a force push"*, which is correct and is the owner's
call regardless.

**I censused 4000 commits on origin/master for splice signatures** (`^commit <40hex>`,
`^Author: `, `^diff --git`, `^index ..`) **and answered 1 in 4000.** Had I stopped there I would
have reported *real mechanism, negligible incidence, no guard warranted*, and that number was
correct about the population it enumerated. **A backticked GIT COMMAND splices output and is
unmissable. A backticked IDENTIFIER — which is what house style actually produces — is
`command not found`, substitutes to EMPTY, and takes its own backticks with it.** There is no
splice to grep for. Second pass, on mid-sentence double-space residue:

    11a846d4d  2026-09-20  40 lines spliced                                 (loud; disclosed)
    f99707dfb  2026-09-20  "agreeing with a  header that means 'partly"     0 backticks
    98ce0b129  2026-09-10  "Gated with  immediately before the demotion."   0 backticks
                           "Every row but  is byte-identical to CPython."   same msg, 2nd hole
    6353e84ec  2026-09-11  SUBJECT LINE: "the fourth  instance"             0 backticks

**Reproduced byte-for-byte:** `MSG="Gated with `-dPXX_NOSELF` immediately before the demotion."`
emits `command not found` to stderr and yields `Gated with  immediately before the demotion.` —
character-identical to `98ce0b129:14`, double space included.

**`98ce0b129` is why this is not cosmetic.** The lost token is recoverable only because
`viaself` happens to appear unbackticked later in the same message, so the line means *"every
row but `viaself` is byte-identical to CPython"* and has read **"every row but is
byte-identical"** for ten days — a load-bearing exception silently deleted from a parity claim.
**Anything without that luck is unrecoverable. Read 4 as a FLOOR**: a lost token whose gap
closes cleanly leaves no residue at all.

**Why care does not fix it.** Git reports success and nobody reads a message back after a green
push, so the failure mode is *never being observed*. And the repo's hooks inspect the command —
by the time one is consulted the shell has already substituted. **But that is true of the
SUBSTITUTION and false of the SPELLING**: `settings.json` registers `PreToolUse`/`Bash` and
`no-variable-rm.sh:49` reads `.tool_input.command`, i.e. the raw string before any shell runs.
**A hook can catch this. It does not need to see the result.** That closed 5b's open fork —
hook, not a post-hoc detector with a different owner.

**AND THE OBVIOUS MATCHER IS BORN RED.** `-m "... `x` ..."` is dangerous and `-m '... `x` ...'`
is safe, and both contain a `-m` and a backtick; a guard denying the second refuses a correct
idiom on its first outside run. **Recommended instead: deny `git commit -m` outright, name `-F`
in the refusal.** No quote-parsing to get subtly wrong, and it closes `<<EOF` for free — which a
backtick matcher does not.

**I wrote "no false-positive population" and 5b measured it: there is one, and it is a TRADE
ACCEPTED, not a trade absent.** The single clause in that recommendation carrying a quantifier
was the one to go measure, which is the rule and which I broke while quoting it all evening.
The population: **`git commit -m '...'` is safe and would be refused**, and `Makefile:36674`,
`Makefile:36860` and `twatch.py:7217` **print `git commit -m '...'` as advice** — a human
copying them is fine, an **agent** copying one into a Bash call is refused by its own tooling's
instruction. Small, real, and it belongs in the refusal text.

**And 5b's narrowing, verified here, which nobody should undo: CONTENT is not the hazard; a
backtick written in the LITERAL SOURCE is.** A backtick arriving inside a *variable* is not
re-evaluated — bash substitutes once, at parse time, not recursively over expanded content:

    added='ticket `whoami` here';  MSG="fix: $added"   ->  fix: ticket `whoami` here   LITERAL
    MSG2="fix: ticket `whoami` here"                    ->  fix: ticket neo here        SUBSTITUTED

So `tools/sync.sh:413` and `tools/file-ticket.sh:126,133` — all double-quoted `-m` with
interpolation — **are safe and must not be "fixed"**. They are also unreachable by the hook
anyway, on the `no-variable-rm` precedent: it sees `tools/sync.sh`, not the `git commit` inside
it. **Which means the guard cannot regress our own tooling — the strongest argument for the
broad form**, and it converts *"no false positives"* into *"false positives bounded, tooling
untouchable"*, a claim that survives someone checking it. **5b was safe all evening by a rule
that said "heredoc, never `-m`", and "heredoc" is the wrong half of that sentence**; it would
have walked into `<<EOF` the first time it wanted a multi-line message. The safe part is the
QUOTED DELIMITER. That is a better argument for the guard than any of the four corrupted
commits, because it shows care does not survive the next shape.

**Routed to 8e (whose finding it is), with 5b standing down explicitly and offering to take it
if 8e declines. I did not build it: a tightening is not the owner's call, but he has taken me
off doing pxx work myself, so it is not mine either.** Positive controls drawn from real
commits, both directions: `6353e84ec` (a SUBJECT-line hit, proving the guard reaches the part
every tool displays) and `98ce0b129` must be REFUSED; a `git commit -F` invocation must be
ACCEPTED, or the guard blocks the remedy it prescribes.

## The relay chain: three explanations, all wrong, none settled by argument

**A relay's correctness is a claim about a MOMENT, and no later tree state on either side can
settle it.** Timeline from the object store and this session's own `.jsonl`:

    16:33:31  0fbc009c2  fixture added; UNWIRED.txt has ZERO matching lines
    16:34:57  MY RELAY   "census rc=1"                      <- 86s after the fixture
    16:35:43  4c8558c32  exemption added                    <- 46s AFTER my relay
    ~16:39    my fetch    origin already at 4c8558c32

- **My rc=1 relay was correct when sent.** There is no tree in which it was not.
- **My self-correction — "stale instrument, 8e fixed it first" — was FALSE.** I saw `origin`
  ahead of `local` and read **my own effect as somebody else's head start**. The fix was 46
  seconds downstream of my message. I then filed the inversion under the failure mode I had
  been primed all evening to hunt. **A stale-instrument error and a correct measurement of an
  earlier tree produce the identical observation**, and the self-blaming reading terminates the
  search.
- **8e's correction was right in verdict and wrong in mechanism.** It argued *"your fetch could
  not have seen a commit my reading of your message caused"* — it could, and did, ~4 minutes
  later. **Second instance tonight of `fae99f4a7`**, roles swapped: its headline was
  object-store fact, checkable on sight, and that is exactly what carried the unmeasured
  ordering claim past me. Better instance than the first for having the roles reversed.

**`git show <sha>:<path>` is airtight about what was true at a commit and cannot answer which
commit someone was standing on when they spoke.** Tree-state evidence cannot settle a timing
dispute however solid it is about trees. The instrument that does is `%cI` on both commits
against the relay's own transcript timestamp — **which every seat holds for every message it
receives and none of us had thought to use once.**

**Nothing false reached the tree.** 8e stopped me logging it with *"a false row in a steering
log is worse than a missing one, because it will be cited"* — which argued against its own
message's mechanism. 5b verified the retraction-of-a-retraction rather than accepting it
(`4714504bb`), recorded the send time as *held by the two seats who have it* rather than
asserting a datum it did not hold, and filed my inversion under *"A PROCESS SEARCH MATCHES ITS
OWN SEARCHER"* rather than as a neighbour: **the observer is a CAUSE, and commit history records
effects without recording who prompted them, so the same inversion arrives with no visible
observer at all.**

## Mine, disclosed

**I ran `git pull --rebase` mid-measurement** — to check whether a peer had written a false row
about me — pulling `4e9ad7e12` into a live `tools-devtest` run whose `progress_*` guards scan
`devdocs/`. **I moved the tree under my own instrument while holding the paragraph that forbids
it.** Killed the run rather than salvage it: a contaminated measurement that survives the
question you ended up asking is indistinguishable from a clean one. Restarted from a settled
tree, recording tree identity at both ends.

**Result: `make tools-devtest` at `4e9ad7e12` — 165 guard(s) green, zero red, rc=0**, identity
`4e9ad7e12`/clean=0 at both ends. **5b's number reproduces at the tip with 8e's exemption in
place.** Census `check_test_wiring.py` rc=0.

**8e's fixture is parked in `test/UNWIRED.txt` with a reason AND a delete-the-exemption
condition on the fixing commit** — because an exemption entry is a countdown and the failure
mode is a countdown quietly becoming a steady state. The header-only version it started with is
exactly `f09f6bcde`, where the instruction lived in a source header, nothing scans one, and the
files sat unwired for six days behind a red that made their own red unreportable.

**Two findings that are the peers', recorded as theirs:** 8e — *the guard caught my file before
any human did, which is its positive control arriving for free; an exemption census that could
not detect a newly unwired file would have passed silently.* **A green census is the one
observation that cannot distinguish a working census from a broken one**, so `165 green` was
unfalsified rather than proven, and real traffic supplied the proof in the only direction that
proves anything. 5b — **de-saturating a job is what converts its future greens from an absence
of evidence into evidence.** Behind a standing red, 8e's file would have been one more
`still_red`.

**And 8e's structural point, which is the half the saturation finding was missing:** it ran its
own gate and the NilPy tier, both green, both honest, and **neither reaches `tools-devtest`**.
*"Not a topic collision between two agents, but a seat unable to see which aggregate its change
lands in."* No per-seat gate closes that by construction.

**LEDGER: unchanged upward.** Nothing escalated, no `decide` filed, **no pin**, **no guard
loosened**, no keystrokes into any pane, no code written by me. Compiler `d9e9b124ee79`.
**Still the owner's:** lekkerzeilen's one unmet row, `assets/facades.lzx`, the four untracked
PNGs, whether to pin. **Standing owner priorities unchanged:** lekkerzeilen compiling and
running; ESP32 prio with hardware coming; profiling explicitly deferred.

### Outcome, same night: the guard landed, and the design beat the recommendation

**`4e9e1c5c8` + `f48347842`, 8e, quote-aware rather than the broad deny I argued for.** It
checked the three advice sites I handed it as a false-positive population and **every one prints
a SINGLE-QUOTED `-m`, which quote-awareness allows** — so the agent-refused-by-its-own-tooling
case does not exist for that design. **The strongest argument for my option was an artefact of my
option.**

**Verified here, not on the report.** `.claude/` diff is **168 insertions, ZERO deletions** —
neither existing hook touched, nothing removed from `settings.json`, **no guard loosened**. 25
controls green, 13 deny / 12 allow, deny cases drawn from commits that really landed corrupted.
Live against the registered hook, both arms: `-m "... $HOME ..."` **refused**, `-m '...'`
**reached git**. **And one negative control neither seat ran: I executed `tools/sync.sh` after
registration** — double-quoted `-m` with interpolation at `:413` — **and it passed**, so
*tooling untouchable* now rests on an exercised path and not only on the `no-variable-rm`
precedent.

**The part worth keeping is what it cost its author.** 8e found that a mark arriving in a
variable is inert, so `"fix: $added"` is safe and its own guard refuses it — **a false positive
in an arm written from the MECHANISM rather than from the code**, which is this file's own
recurring failure, discovered by the author against himself. Rather than drop the arm he
censused what it protects: `$(PXX_STABLE)` x83, `$i` x42, `$PT` x32, plus `$DD`, `$else`,
`$Integer` — **ordinary prose in this repo, every one silently deleted if written into a
double-quoted message.** The dollar arms protect an order of magnitude more than the backtick
arm, **and the backtick arm is the one we had the corpse for.** So the false positive is
promoted to an **asserted control with both populations in the header**: a later seat prices the
trade instead of rediscovering it and filing it as a bug.

**And he declined to teach it quoted heredocs while it was refusing his own commits** — *"adding
parsing to reduce my own friction risks a false NEGATIVE on a silent permanent corruption, which
is the wrong direction to be wrong in."* He used the prescribed Write-tool path twice instead of
rephrasing prose to slip past, **the single move the guard forbids, declined by its own author
under friction.** `no-variable-rm.sh`'s header earned its line: it cannot tell a description
from an invocation **and must not try**.

**The control output asserts its own blind spot** — *"NOT covered, asserted so nobody assumes
it: 1 heredoc case"*. **A guard that states where it does not look cannot have that mistaken for
coverage**, which is exactly the green-census failure 8e diagnosed earlier the same night,
closed in the commit that created the risk.

### The pin question was the wrong question: reachability is about the CONSUMER

**I asked whether a pin carried tonight's NilPy fixes, which was the right check and the wrong
question.** Confirmed both ways: pin v413 (`d79e66f07`) carries none of `fb0c0af11`,
`12806ea63`, `a60a8daa0`. **But the demo is not pin-gated at all**, so that sentence was about
a population the demo is not in.

**`runbin.sh:102-104` is the whole finding:**

    PXX_ROOT=${PXX_ROOT:-/home/neo/frank-user}
    latest) PXX="$PXX_ROOT/compiler/pascal26"

**The default `PXX_ROOT` is the OWNER'S OWN CHECKOUT.** Measured read-only: it sits at
`d79e66f07` with `compiler/pascal26` = `f94c2a7e2396d2be`, **byte-identical to the sha in that
pin commit's own subject**, and none of the three fixes are ancestors of its HEAD. 8e ran the
command he would type rather than an equivalent one, one variable changed:

    PXX_ROOT=/home/neo/frank-user  (his, the DEFAULT)  -> TypeError: object is not callable, rc=217
    PXX_ROOT=/home/neo/frankB      (has the fixes)     -> sails, rc=0, 0.9 kn

**The `TypeError` is blocker 03's signature error and the same string the PINNED binary gives on
the 03 fixture** — two instruments agreeing about which compiler he is on.

**SO "LEKKERZEILEN COMPILES AND WORKS TONIGHT" WAS NOT TRUE FOR HIM, AND IT WAS ONE REPORT AWAY
FROM BEING SAID.** It was true about a seat's own tree, which is not the tree that matters. **The
reach of every fix tonight is a `git pull` and a REBUILD in his checkout, not a pin** — and
nobody may do it for him, because pulling or rebuilding in another seat's tree is not a thing to
do unasked.

**GENERAL FORM, 8e's, and CLAUDE.md names no rule for it:** *"is it pinned"* is a question about
an **artefact**; **reachability is a question about a CONSUMER.** Every consumer resolves its
compiler its own way, and a default buried in a demo's runner script is a resolution path no
rule here names. **Read the consumer's own resolution — and where the consumer is a PERSON, run
the command THEY would run rather than an equivalent one**, because a default `PXX_ROOT` is
exactly what an equivalent command supplies correctly and the real one does not. **A seat
reporting "fixed and landed" is reporting about its own tree.**

**A pull alone is not enough** — it leaves the old binary on disk, which is this file's own
five-routes-to-a-stale-binary problem arriving at the one person who cannot debug it. The
rebuild is ~12s and is the step that matters.

### Where this fleet loses work: a bug filed in an application repo is invisible both ways

**8e's observation, measured here because it is this lane's question.** A compiler bug filed only
in an application's repo is invisible to `ready`, the ranker and Track T going IN — **and the
fix is invisible coming OUT**, because nothing connects a landed fix back to the application's
document.

**The seven lekkerzeilen blockers against every ticket folder, matching each blocker's slug:**

    01 field-from-qualified-float-const               2 tickets
    02 pop-as-argument-stringifies                    0
    03 field-shadows-another-class-method             0
    04 property-setter-skipped-through-bare-receiver  2 tickets
    05 type-of-shadowed-method-jumps-to-null          0
    06 unused-import-changes-resolution               0  (1 under a shorter spelling)
    07 nested-import-guard-compiles-the-dead-arm      6 tickets

**INSTRUMENT BOUND, AND IT CHANGES WHAT THE ZEROS MEAN: this matches SLUG TEXT, so it proves
absence-of-NAME and not absence-of-TICKET.** A ticket describing the same mechanism in other
words does not match — and one did: the bare-read work has a ticket the first pass missed.
**Read the zeros as "no ticket names this."**

**The live casualty is 05:** open, on the owner's named priority, worked all night, and **named
by no ticket** — so it cannot be ranked, cannot be dispatched, and if the seat holding it stops,
it stops.

**AND THE TWO HALVES ARE INDEPENDENT, WHICH 07 PROVES: it had SIX tickets — the best-tracked of
the seven — and still sat FIXED WITH A STALE DOCUMENT all day**, on the goal the owner had
named, because tracking the BUG never connected the FIX to the DOC. **Being well-tracked did not
protect it.** Fixing the going-in half would not have touched the coming-out half.

**Same animal as the demo not running on his checkout:** work that exists and does not reach the
person it was for. Two instances in one evening, both found by the seat in the repo, both after
this lane asked an artefact question and stopped at the artefact answer.

**No machinery proposed.** Recorded measured so the pattern can accumulate rather than be
rediscovered.

#### CORRECTION, same night: the census above is WRONG in its headline, and the bound is what caught it

**"Invisible to `ready`, the ranker and Track T" is FALSE.**
`devdocs/progress/backlog-umbrella/umbrella-lekkerzeilen-compiles-and-runs-under-nilpy.md`
exists at **prio 90 with 16 `blocked-by` edges**, created 2026-09-08 by the owner. **The
prescribed mechanism is in place and in use** — membership is an edge, `effective_prio` inherits
the max, and the demo's blockers are ranked through it. I censused for the DEMO REPO'S slug
names, found none, and concluded the work was untracked. **It is tracked under this repo's own
naming, which is the correct naming.**

**And 05's zero was false too.** The ticket is
`bug-n-type-of-a-member-read-on-a-bare-receiver-jumps-through-a-null-pointer-in-the-lekkerzeilen-demo`,
prio 45, open. **The stated instrument bound — "it proves absence-of-NAME, not absence-of-TICKET"
— is the only reason this was caught**, and it was caught by the seat I handed the finding to,
in the same message that carried the zeros. **A census reporting a gap on the owner's named
priority, with a clear call to act, is about as persuasive as an instrument gets**; what defused
it was the author saying what the instrument could not see, in the same breath. Had the bound
not been there, a duplicate ticket would have split an open bug's history in two.

**WHAT SURVIVES, NARROWER AND SHARPER: 05 is not wired to that umbrella — and it SHOULD NOT BE.**
Wiring it would inherit `effective_prio` 90 and put it at the top of Track N's queue. **It does
not block the demo:** the probe line is not demo code and the demo runs without it. 8e held prio
at 45 deliberately and the ticket now says why. **My push to raise its visibility would have been
wrong**, and wrong in the expensive direction — *"ranking a ticket on the repo it was found in
rather than on what it stops is how the float folder got 26 entries."*

**THE 07 HALF SURVIVES UNTOUCHED AND IS NOW THE WHOLE FINDING.** Six tickets named it, the
best-tracked of the seven, **and it still sat FIXED WITH A STALE DOCUMENT all day on the goal the
owner named.** Tracking the BUG never connected the FIX to the DOC. **Being well-tracked did not
protect it** — which is why the coming-out half is real even though the going-in half, as I
stated it, was not.

**The general lesson is against me and it is the evening's own shape a sixth time:** I measured
a naming convention and reported a governance failure. **The instrument was honest about slugs
and I asked it a question about visibility.** The population it enumerated could not contain the
answer, because the answer is an EDGE in a file whose name shares no token with the thing it
tracks.

### Two operational facts every seat needs, both verified here

**1. COMMITTING IN `/home/neo/lekkerzeilen` PUBLISHES TO GITHUB.** `.git/hooks/post-commit` is an
owner-installed auto-push (his decision 2026-09-19, hook dated 09-19 17:34): every commit on
`main` goes to `origin` in the background, never forced, failures logged not retried. **It
fires** — `.git/autopush.log` shows three pushes today. **So a commit in that clone is an
OUTWARD-FACING act**, which this file elsewhere says needs the owner's say-so; he has given it,
once, for that repo. **Know it before you commit there.** Benign side effect worth knowing:
an explicit `git push` afterwards reports *"Everything up-to-date"* because the hook already
sent it, **which reads exactly like a failed push if you do not know the hook exists.**

**2. MEASURE AGAINST THE PIN, NOT AGAINST A PEER'S CHECKOUT.** Three seats read
`/home/neo/frank-user` at three times tonight and **all three relayed a reading as a property**:
`d79e66f07`/`f94c2a7e` at 16:39, a different claim at ~11:57, and `7caadced1`/`55f1ef09` at
18:21 after the owner pulled and rebuilt. Every relay was true when taken and false when read.
**This is the tense half of the scope-word rule arriving at a TREE instead of a seat — and a
tree has no transcript to check against.**

**The repair is not to read his tree more carefully; it is to stop reading it.**
`stable_linux_amd64/default/pinned` is `f94c2a7e2396d2be` — **a fixed artefact with a quotable
sha, present in every checkout, that cannot drift.** 8e re-took every pin-v413 claim against it:

    01 repro               -> 1700   (README says error)
    02, the three str rows -> Mesh, Mesh, Mesh
    07 nestguard           -> byte-identical to HEAD
    bare-read fixture      -> SIGSEGV, rc=139

**The SIGSEGV row is what makes the other three mean anything:** v413 demonstrably carries three
of the four fixes and not the fourth, **so the greens are discriminating rather than polite.** An
instrument that passed everything pointed at it would never have been shown able to fail. Anchored
at `2636ff1` (demo repo) and `da38aadc4` (here), re-derivable by anyone, forever, without
stat-ing a live tree.

**And 8e checked whether its own demo build caused the rebuild it was measuring** — mtimes were
18:21 and 18:21 — establishing from `runbin.sh:129` that the runner only INVOKES the compiler out
of `PXX_ROOT` and never builds there. **"My measurement created the condition it was measuring"
is the one that would have poisoned the evening**, and it was excluded by reading the script
rather than by assuming.

### Correction to my own census: a shared CONSTANT is a handle, never evidence of a shared defect

**I published an 18-row census of boolean deciders scanning against `MainProgramTokCount` and
ranked `PyDefUsedAsValue` first. The ranking was wrong and the census needs a limit printed on
it.**

**`MainProgramTokCount` DOES NOT MEAN "THE MAIN FILE".** Its own header says the scan starts at 0
so *"a read in an EARLIER-lexed module is in range and a read in a later one is not"* — **an
order-dependent high-water mark.** Confirmed with the compiler's own probe, `PXXDBG=n.dval`:
window `[236024, 236039)` against `tokCount=236094` — **55 tokens genuinely beyond the scan, a
module-side value use among them, not normalised, and the program still correct.** **The name is
not the thing, in a constant, and it misled two seats in one evening** — both of us read the
identifier and inferred the semantics.

**So a grep for a shared constant finds every function that MENTIONS it and cannot tell which
QUESTION each is asking.** The constant is an excellent way to BUILD a candidate list — it
reached 8e's two repaired functions in one command where *"what else answers this question"* has
no mechanical form — **and it is not evidence of a shared defect.** Both halves belong together,
**or someone widens sixteen scans on the strength of a grep.**

**AND THE RANKING FAILED FOR A REASON WORTH MORE THAN THE ROW.** I ranked `PyDefUsedAsValue`
first because its own header names 8e's exact failure mode — `call_ind` jumping to the variant's
tag word, called **fatal**. Four probes: no crash, including that case, returning `12.0`.
**The comment was not stale and I was not misreading it as stale — the "fatal" describes the
OPPOSITE FAILURE DIRECTION.** It documents `bug-nilpy-import-name-forces-function-object-abi`,
where the scan counted too MUCH:

    the three confirmed:  a MISS is a crash,       a false hit costs boxing
    PyDefUsedAsValue:     a miss costs boxing,     a FALSE HIT is the crash

**Widening its scan would risk CAUSING the documented fatal bug, not fixing one. Both of us read
a comment warning against the change we were contemplating as evidence FOR it** — because we
arrived already holding three confirmed instances, and the comment named the right crash at the
right call site. **A warning that names your failure mode reads as corroboration when you are
looking for siblings, and the direction is the one thing a shape-match cannot carry.** Ask which
way a documented failure runs before counting it as support.

**The row is retired WITH A REASON rather than as cannot-reproduce**, which shrinks the decider
population and therefore strengthens the argument that `pybound_new_star` should stop trusting
an unverifiable precondition — it has fewer callers to be wrong about than the census suggested.

### No measurement recovers INTENT — and a correct reading of the code reported a defect that is not one

**The owner, asked about the lambda finding:** *"i do recall there were cases we could not compile
lambda's or similar constructs, and **i told your sis agent to just use eval in such case**. it's
also why eval matured significant, because it can do pretty much anything."*

**The pyeval closure is a designed escape hatch he ORDERED.** Its capability is a direct
consequence of that instruction — it had to cover what the compiled path could not, so it does.
**So *"it falls back to eval"* is not a defect and must not be filed as one.** The live question
is only whether the LIFTED path could have taken this lambda, and the lever is the line already
in the tree: `pyparser.inc:11080`, **the bound-fn bridge DISCARDS the callee's result**, which is
why every value-returning lambda keeps the interpreter. ***Widen the lift; leave eval alone.***
If that is expensive it is a trade to price, not a wrong to right.

**NOTHING IN THE TREE RECORDS THAT THE FALLBACK WAS INSTRUCTED RATHER THAN MERELY IMPLEMENTED,
AND NO INSTRUMENT CAN RECOVER THAT.** The comments describe *what* it does, accurately and in
detail; **they cannot say a human asked for it.** So a seat reading the code finds a slow path
and reports a defect **correctly and wrongly at the same time** — and every instrument agrees
with it: the comment is honest, the cost is real, the mechanism is exactly as documented. **This
is not the usual failure of an instrument answering about the wrong population. The population
is right, the reading is right, and the missing datum is not in the repository at all.**

That is CLAUDE.md's *"reconstruct the record; ask him for the reasoning behind it"* paying out:
**one line of his time changed the FIX, not just the tone.**

**MINE, AND IT IS THE PART WORTH KEEPING: I said "by design" in one clause and then built the
whole message around a cost mechanism and a ~7x multiplier.** A sentence pointing at the bridge,
wrapped in a paragraph pointing at the interpreter — **and the paragraph is what a reader acts
on.** Had 8e filed from my message it would have filed against the hatch. **A correct
qualification does not survive a frame that contradicts it**, and the frame is carried by which
thing the message spends its length on, not by which clause is technically right. **Check what
your message is ABOUT, not only whether its sentences are true.**

## A PEER'S SENT-MESSAGE RECORD IS A RECORD OF ATTEMPTS. DELIVERY IS ONLY RECORDED AT THE FAR END.

**franks-5b audited my `24c2f18de` against its own sends rather than agreeing with it — the right
move — and quoted a message `to frankz (relay hop)` carrying `104-109 fps`. It is not in my
transcript.** 46 inbound messages from 5b, one socket (`97533.sock`), no rename, 09-19 to 09-20;
the cap claim appears in exactly two, both tonight and both after the dispute. Checked
`open water`, `104`, `capped`, `above 60`, `invisible` across all 46; the loose `10[0-9]-10[0-9]`
hits before 17:16 are line ranges.

**It pulled every SendMessage in its transcript and read the `to:` field. That population is what a
seat TRIED to send.** Whether a message arrived is recorded only at the receiver. Not a claim that
its send failed — I hold no evidence either way and it holds the `tool_result` that would say. **The
coordination consequence is the one to keep: when two seats reconstruct an exchange, the sender's
record and the receiver's record are DIFFERENT instruments, and only the pair settles anything.**
Second time tonight a seat reasoned confidently from a record that was airtight about the wrong
thing.

**AND THE AUDIT FOUND A MISATTRIBUTION IN MY OWN ENTRY.** Both numbers reached me **first-hand from
`lekkerzeilen-7a` at 10:40**, in 7a's own table, labelled *"vsync off in both arms"* — seven hours
before the relay. So *"second-hand through franks-5b"* was **first-hand knowledge relabelled as
hearsay, in the direction that transfers the duty to check it**, and I quoted that sentence into the
playbook as the exemplary half eleven minutes after writing it. **A hedge reads as modesty, so nobody
audits one, least of all its author.** Corrected to 7a directly, since it is 7a's measurement.

**The mechanism, finer than hedge attrition and banked at `f2292c03f`:** one sentence, one source
table — *"region ~2.6 fps VSYNC-OFF, far below any cap; open water 104-109, above 60 and therefore
capped WITH VSYNC ON."* **The condition was kept where it is inert and inverted where it decides the
answer** (vsync-ON open water is 14.6). The region half was being QUOTED, the open-water half
CONCLUDED FROM. **5b made the identical swap on the identical row independently the same evening**,
so *"send the number, not the conclusion"* is not the discharge: the number was sent, correctly
labelled, by its author, to both of us. **Put the condition inside the clause that draws the
inference. An inert label is the one that survives, so seeing one carried correctly is no evidence
the live one was.**

**Collision protocol from tonight applied rather than rediscovered:** told 5b I was editing the
neighbouring section before doing it, instead of finding out 221 seconds later.

## A SEND RECEIPT IS NOT A DELIVERY, AND MY COUNT OF DELIVERIES WAS ONE OF TWO PATHS. I NEARLY
## RAISED A FLEET ALARM ON A SYSTEM THAT WORKS.

**franks-5b's record said 68 sends to me, all `success:true`; mine said 46 inbound. Delta 22.**
Neither of us could close it alone, and 5b conceded the right half unprompted: **a `SendMessage` in
your own transcript records an ATTEMPT.** Delivery is recorded only at the receiver.

**THE RECEIVER'S RECORD HAS TWO PATHS AND I WAS COUNTING ONE.** An inbound message is enqueued, then
either delivered as a standalone `user`-role turn **or ABSORBED INTO A TURN ALREADY RUNNING** — the
queue entry removed with `reason: absorbed_mid_turn`, reappearing as `type: attachment` /
`attachment.type: queued_command`. My filter matched only the standalone shape.

    franks-5b   enqueued 69   absorbed 23   standalone 46   ->  69 (5b's 68 + tonight's in-flight)
    FLEET       enqueued 484  absorbed 198  standalone 286  ->  absorbed+standalone == enqueued,
                                                                exactly, for all 22 senders

**Nothing has ever been dropped.** Zero genuine delivery notices; no prior frankZ transcript has seen
5b's socket, so the restart candidate is dead too.

**THE NEAR-MISS IS THE ENTRY, NOT THE MECHANISM.** I had the table built and phrased — *"41% of
inbound to the coordinator is being dropped, two senders at 100%"* — and it was one command from
going to frankuser and the fleet, on the owner's last ~9% of weekly budget. **It would have been
confident, well-evidenced, arithmetically consistent, and about a system working correctly.**
What stopped it was the `reason` field on a record I had already read twice.

**AND THE THING THAT SHOULD HAVE STOPPED IT SOONER IS WHAT I READ AS CONFIRMATION: `enq - rem =
delivered` held for EVERY sender, with no residue.** I took a perfect fit across 22 populations as
corroboration. **A partition that sums exactly is evidence the two halves are ONE population, not
that one half is missing** — a real loss channel is lossy, not arithmetically perfect. **Suspect a
clean identity; a mechanism that drops messages does not balance to the message.**

**Third seat tonight to reason from a record airtight about the wrong thing, and this one was mine.**
5b reasoned from attempts; 7a verified a relay's CONTENT and not its PROVENANCE; I counted one of two
delivery paths. Same class, three different neighbouring questions.

**Fleet-useful residue:** a message to a BUSY seat lands mid-turn, not behind it, and generates no
notice either way. Send freely; expect it to arrive in the middle of something.

## DECISION: TSP'S BACKEND IS NOT PORTED IN THIS WINDOW. AND I PUT A WRONG NUMBER IN THE SIZING
## SEAT'S HANDS BY TRANSPLANTING A FILE SIZE INTO A COST QUESTION.

**The owner, tonight, via frankuser, and this is a DECISION and not a preference:** *"yes for now i
rather keep it as is. both lekkerzeilen and TSP run fine under CPython and Linux. we are good and
should not overcomplicate. after all, 10 days counting down."*

**So: each demo keeps its own `platform/` layer, and pxx does NOT absorb `_pxx.py` as a general
loader.** He raised the general-loader idea himself and talked himself out of it; frankuser agreed
with the cost named — **shipping it means owning a third-party API surface and its version skew
forever, for two demos.** Banked by frankuser with the reasoning attached. **Not to be re-opened as a
fresh idea next week**, which is the failure mode a decision without its reasoning invites.

**The deferred fork underneath it, on the record and NOT on anyone's queue:** do Python programs reach
C libraries through **ctypes**, or through pxx's own **C-header import**? *A fat hand-written binding
is evidence the import is not carrying enough.* Real Track U question; explicitly not this window's.

**MY ERROR, CORRECTED TO THE SEAT THAT HELD IT.** I told c0 that lekkerzeilen's `platform/_pxx.py` at
**~974 lines** was *"your best estimate for what TSP's port actually costs"*. **974 is the SIZE OF A
FILE** — a figure that exists in CLAUDE.md for an unrelated reason, to record a ticket calling that
file *"a 39-line stub"* after the owner rewrote it. frankuser measured the real quantity: the two
layers are the same design file-for-file, diffs of **27 / 56 / 40 / 202 lines**. Copy-and-adapt.
**Out by roughly an order of magnitude, in the direction that makes work look bigger, already in the
hands of the seat doing the sizing.**

**Structurally corroborated here in one command, which is the cheap check I should have run first:**

    lekkerzeilen/platform/  __init__.py _ctypes_backend.py _gl.py _pxx.py _sdl2.py _vocab.py
    tsp/platform/           __init__.py _ctypes_backend.py _gl.py         _sdl2.py _vocab.py

**File-for-file identical, one file apart.** That is the whole port, and it was one `ls` away.

**This is tonight's own mechanism in a second subsystem, committed by the seat that landed the rule
two hours earlier:** a true measurement carried into a question it was not measured for, with the
number crossing unchanged while what licenses it does not. **A size is not a cost.** Extended the
playbook section rather than adding a neighbour.

**BUDGET FRAME FOR EVERYTHING ROUTED FROM HERE, frankuser's:** **~10 days of max tokens, and that
window IS the release window.** After it, max tokens return only with a sponsor. **Prefer work that
produces durable knowledge over work that produces a fix someone could make later** — c0's committed
inventory named as the model.

## THE DIFF-COUNT ROW IS REFUTED, NOT CARRIED — AND THE TEST THAT SEPARATES A LEGITIMATE EXACT FIT
## FROM THE ONE THAT NEARLY COST ME A FLEET ALARM IS c0's.

**frankuser's `27/56/40/202` is withdrawn by its author with the defect named:**
`diff -u | grep -c '^[+-][^+-]'` — the class **requires a character after the sign**, so a changed
BLANK line (a bare `+` or `-`) never matches. Written to skip the `---`/`+++` headers; it silently
drops every blank line added or removed. **Verified here on lekkerzeilen `25e188f` clean:**

    file          plain   frankuser's   blank-only   sum
    _gl.py          30        27             3        30
    _sdl2.py        67        56            11        67
    _vocab.py       41        40             1        41
    __init__.py    217       202            15       217

**THIS IS WHERE THE FILE'S "CARRY BOTH ROWS" RULE DOES NOT APPLY, AND THE DISTINCTION IS WORTH MORE
THAN THE NUMBERS.** Carry both when a re-run disagrees and nobody can say why. **Here the why is
named, mechanistic, and was given a chance to fail on a tree its author never touched** — running
frankuser's command on MY checkout could have produced anything and produced its exact set. One row
refuted by mechanism; replace it.

**c0's TEST, WHICH IS THE PROMOTABLE THING: *what would this number be if the thing I am testing were
BROKEN?*** My 22-sender partition (`enq - rem = delivered`, all 22, no residue) would have summed
exactly **whatever the delivery layer did** — I could not describe the world in which it fails.
**A conservation identity over a closed accounting is ZERO evidence, not weak evidence.** The
blank-line account is the opposite: a prediction with a failure mode, exercised independently.
**Both are exact arithmetic fits across every row and only one is evidence.** c0's own instance in its
own subsystem: `blockAlign == sampwidth` on mono — **asserting a product of two things that are the
same thing**, which is why breaking the shim left the suite at 61/61.

**AND c0's PROVENANCE FORM SUPERSEDES MINE FROM EARLIER TONIGHT.** I wrote that a number with a
plausible source stops the next person measuring. c0's is sharper and actionable: **everyone already
cites sources, and a citation is what PROTECTS a number from being re-checked — so cite the QUESTION
the source was measured for.** `974` cited a real file; what it never cited is that nobody had ever
measured a port with it. **A naked number gets challenged BECAUSE it is naked.**

**ROUTING, and I am recording that I did not write it:** frankuser asked that the partial-match rule
be **c0's** to write, since c0 caught the live one — its `_gl.py` hit matching a wrong method exactly
while `_sdl2.py` gave 6 against 56. Headline kept verbatim: ***"a partial match is not partial
evidence; it is full confidence for the part that agrees, spent on the part that does not."*** Four
instances, four subsystems, one evening. **The recurrence bar is cleared by count, not by argued
quality, which is the test this file asks for.**

**A NON-CHANGE RECORDED AS A DECISION, c0's, endorsed:** `readframes`/`writeframes` keep byte-by-byte
copies through `TPyBytes.put`/`at` rather than a bulk `Move` — verified green in three runtimes,
clips ~100 KB, sub-millisecond either way. **Changing verified code to save a time nobody can measure
is the trade this tree warns about.** The unit comment names buffering as the cost, which is where a
future large-file caller should look.
